# Fel Dwarf Bonus <br> Creature Compilation 3.0
The following is a collection of Fel Dwarf-themed creatures. This list is a supplement to the Fel Dwarf Compendium. The creatures here can all be used on their own, but will complement the creatures from the Fel Dwarf Compendium thematically and mechanically.

## Fel Dwarf Artificers


Among the merciless society of the Fel Dwarfs, infernal artificers exemplify the cruel ingenuity born from such a culture. Only the demonsmiths and sorcerer-prophets surpass them in crafting weapons of suffering. These hellish artisans are vital to the ceaseless creation of war machines that fuel the ambitious conquests of the Fel Dwarfs.

Although they do not wield brutal sorcery like their demonsmith masters, Fel Dwarf artificers possess extensive knowledge of the dark arts, alchemy, and metallurgy. They use this expertise to fabricate handheld versions of their unholy siege weapons—devices so cursed that only the artificers themselves can wield them. While it may seem that these contraptions are restricted to their creators to prevent their use against the Fel Dwarfs, the dark truth is far grimmer. During the forging process, a portion of the artificer’s soul is bound to the weapon, creating a dangerous connection. Even after the weapon's completion, its use erodes the wielder's soul and body, with prolonged exposure leading to horrific mutations or gradual petrification from within.


### Doomquake Battle Smith
The doomquake battle maul incorporates the devastating power of the doomquake mortar into a two-handed weapon. Artificers who wield these weapons in battle are a destructive force, capable of toppling stone walls with shockwaves that ripple out from every strike, pulverizing everything in their path. These weapons can also be slammed into the ground, causing the earth to buckle and shatter. Those unfortunate enough to stand on the unstable surface are swallowed by a churning sea of roiling earth.

### Magma Cannoneer
Using the same twisted alchemy as the magma cannon, magma spewers deliver gruesome deaths on the battlefield. These contraptions consist of a canister strapped to the artificer's back, constantly emitting black fumes and a sinister hissing sound. Tubes extend from the canister to a large nozzle, held by the artificer, which sprays molten metal capable of engulfing an entire regiment in seconds.

\columnbreak

### Deathscreecher Artillerist
Deathscreecher artillerists bombard their enemies with blasphemous ammunition derived from deathscreecher rockets. These Fel Dwarfs load handheld mortars with demolition rockets capable of felling a hill giant on impact. Alternatively, they can choose the more wicked dreadfire rockets, which release a hord of burning souls. These spectral entities howl through the air, seeking out the nearest living beings to incinerate.



### Fel Dwarf Artificer Tactics

The following tips can help you run fel dwarf artificers in combat more effectively:

#### Doomquake Battle Smith
* Excels at disrupting melee combatants and pairs well with ranged allies.
* Uses Doomquake Slam to create difficult terrain:
    * Position the slam so enemies are near the far edge of the area as this hinders enemy movement toward the battle smith and allies.

#### Magma Spewer
* Targets tightly packed groups of enemies, especially those with low Dexterity.
* Performs exceptionally well in chokepoints:
    * Use Resolute Stance to hold the line effectively.

#### Deathscreecher Artillerist
* Seeks opportunities to:
    *  Target three or more enemies.
    *  Target at least two enemies with low Wisdom using the Frighten ability.
* Forces enemies within 60 feet of the cannon to flee.
    *  Be cautious not to let allies pursue fleeing enemies into danger.
*  Uses Demolition Rocket in situations where crowd control is not possible.

> ## Fel Dwarf Doomquake Battle Smith
> *Medium humanoid (dwarf), lawful evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 17 (splint)  
> - **Hit Points** 105 (14d8 + 42)  
> - **Speed** 25 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |14 (+2)|8 (-1)|16 (+3)|16 (+3)|12 (+1)|10 (+0)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Strength +5, Intelligence +6  
> - **Damage Resistances** fire, poison  
> - **Skills** Arcana +6, Athletics +5, Land Vehicles +3, Smith's Tools +3, Tinker's Tools +3  
> - **Senses** darkvision 30 ft., passive Perception 10  
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal, Goblin, Orc  
> - **Challenge** 6 (2,300 XP)   &emsp;&emsp; **Proficiency Bonus** +3  
> ___
> 
> ***Debilitating Strikes.*** When the dwarf reduces a creature to 0 hit points with an attack, that creature is stunned for the next minute, even if it regains hit points.  
> 
> ***Dwarven Resilience.*** The dwarf has advantage on saving throws against poison.  
> 
> ***Siege Monster.*** The dwarf deals double damage to objects and structures.  
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Multiattack.*** The dwarf makes two attacks.  
> 
> ***Doomquake Maul.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage plus 9 (2d8) fire damage. If the target is a creature, it becomes sheathed in booming energy until the start of the dwarf's next turn. If the target willingly moves 5 feet or more before then, it takes 9 (2d8) thunder damage, and the energy dissipates.  
> 
> ***Doomquake Slam (3/Day).*** The dwarf slams the ground with its Doomquake Maul, creating a 15-foot cube of burning earth originating from itself. Each creature in the cube must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage, 9 (2d8) fire damage, and is knocked prone. On a success, a creature takes half damage and isn't knocked prone. The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain until cleared and the area vibrates violently until the start of the dwarf's next turn. At that point, the vibrations end in a calamitous burst, and each creature in the area must make a DC 14 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 14 (4d6) thunder damage. On a success, it takes half damage. 
>
> ### Bonus Actions  
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf’s turn, it becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
>
> **New condition described below*



### Doomquake Battle Smith Upgrade

The Doomquake Battle Smith can have any of the following upgrades. Its challenge rating (CR) increases by 1 for each upgrade it has.

##### Doomquake Battle Smith Upgrade
| d3  | Upgrade |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Deafening Thunder.***  Creatures sheathed in booming energy or standing in a vibrating area are deafened and cannot cast spells with a verbal component. A creature that fails a Constitution saving throw suffers these effects until the end of the dwarf’s next turn.
|  2  | ***Shockwave.***  The area of the Doomquake Slam increases to a 60-foot-long, 15-foot-wide line originating from the dwarf.
|  3  | ***Seismic Snare.*** When a creature fails the Dexterity saving throw against the Doomquake Slam while standing on earth or stone, it becomes buried as it sinks into the ground. A buried creature is restrained. Any creature, including the buried one, can use its action to free the buried creature by succeeding on a DC 14 Strength check. A creature making this check has advantage if it has a burrow speed.|

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
> * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
> * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
The Resolute condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

\pagebreakNum

> ## Fel Dwarf Magma Cannoneer
> *Medium humanoid (dwarf), lawful evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 17 (splint)  
> - **Hit Points** 97 (13d8 + 39)  
> - **Speed** 25 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |14 (+2)|8 (-1)|16 (+3)|16 (+3)|12 (+1)|10 (+0)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Constitution +6, Intelligence +6  
> - **Damage Resistances** fire, poison  
> - **Skills** Arcana +6, Athletics +5, Land Vehicles +3, Smith's Tools +3, Tinker's Tools +3
> - **Senses** darkvision 30 ft., passive Perception 10  
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal, Goblin, Orc  
> - **Challenge** 5 (1,800 XP)   &emsp;&emsp; **Proficiency Bonus** +3  
> ___
>
> ***Debilitating Strikes.*** When the dwarf reduces a creature to 0 hit points with an attack, that creature is stunned for the next minute, even if it regains hit points.  
> 
> ***Dwarven Resilience.*** The dwarf has advantage on saving throws against poison.  
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Fire Splash.*** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, range 60/90 ft., two targets with in 5 feet of each other. *Hit:* 16 (3d10) fire damage.  
>
> ***Magma Spray (3/Day).*** The dwarf sprays molten metal in a 30-foot cone. Each creature in the cone must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 16 (3d10) fire damage and ignites. On a success, a creature takes half damage and isn't ignited. An ignited creature takes 5 (1d10) fire damage at the end of each of its turns until it uses an action to douse the flames.  
>
> ### Bonus Actions  
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf’s turn, it becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
>
> **New condition described on previous page*


### Magma Cannoneer Upgrade

The magma cannoneer can have any of the following upgrades. Its challenge rating (CR) increases by 1 for each upgrade it has.

##### Magma Cannoneer Upgrade
| d3  | Upgrade |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Caustic Tar.*** A creature ignited by the Magma Spray ability is also restrained. If a creature uses its action to douse the flames, it must succeed on a DC 14 Strength check. On a failure, the flames are not extinguished.
|  2  | ***Pyretic Acid.***  The magma cannoneer now deals acid damage instead of fire damage. Whenever a creature takes acid damage from the magma cannoneer, any armor it is wearing takes a permanent and cumulative -1 penalty to the AC it offers. Armor reduced to an AC of 10 is destroyed.
|  3  | ***Stinking Sulfur.*** Each creature that starts its turn within 5 feet of an ignited creature must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution saving throw or be poisoned until the end of its current turn. While poisoned in this way, the creature is Dazed (see new condition) and can’t cast spells other than cantrips.|

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Dazed 
> A dazed creature can’t take reactions and on its turn, it must choose whether it gets a move, an action, or a bonus action; it gets only one of the three.

> ## Fel Dwarf Deathscreecher Artillerist
> *Medium humanoid (dwarf), lawful evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 17 (splint)  
> - **Hit Points** 97 (13d8 + 39)  
> - **Speed** 25 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |14 (+2)|8 (-1)|16 (+3)|16 (+3)|12 (+1)|10 (+0)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Wisdom +4, Intelligence +6  
> - **Damage Resistances** fire, poison  
> - **Skills** Arcana +6, Athletics +5, Land Vehicles +3, Smith's Tools +3, Tinker's Tools +3  
> - **Senses** darkvision 30 ft., passive Perception 10  
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal, Goblin, Orc  
> - **Challenge** 5 (1,800 XP)   &emsp;&emsp; **Proficiency Bonus** +3  
> ___
>
> ***Debilitating Strikes.*** When the dwarf reduces a creature to 0 hit points with an attack, that creature is stunned for the next minute, even if it regains hit points.  
> 
> ***Dwarven Resilience.*** The dwarf has advantage on saving throws against poison.  
> <br> <br>
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Demolition Rocket.*** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, range 120/320 ft., one target. *Hit:* 30 (5d10 + 3) piercing damage. If the target is an object or structure, it takes double damage. The dwarf can’t use this attack again until it reloads the launcher as an action or by using its Resolute Stance feature (no action required).  
>
> ***Dread Fire Rocket (3/Day).*** The dwarf fires a cursed rocket from its launcher at a point of its choice within 120 feet but no less than 15 feet away. The rocket explodes, creating a 15-foot-radius sphere of burning souls. At the start of the dwarf's next turn, the sphere moves up to 30 feet toward the nearest creature before disappearing. 
>
> Any creature in the sphere when it appears or creature whose space the sphere moves into must make a DC 14 Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 10 (3d6) fire damage and is frightened until the end of the dwarf's next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage and isn't frightened. If the creature fails the save by 5 or more (DC 9), it can't take reactions and must take the Dash action on each of its turns to move away from the sphere by the safest available route. 
> 
> The dwarf can't use this ability again until it reloads the launcher as an action or by using its Resolute Stance feature (no action required).  
> 
> ### Bonus Actions  
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf’s turn, it becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
>
> **New condition described on page before last*



### Deathscreecher Artillerist Upgrade

The deathscreecher artillerist can have any of the following upgrades. Its challenge rating (CR) increases by 1 for each upgrade it has.

##### Deathscreecher Artillerist Upgrades
| d3  | Upgrade |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Fear-Fueled Inferno.***  If a creature that is already frightened makes a saving throw against the Dread Fire Rocket, it has disadvantage on the saving throw and takes double the fire damage on a failure.
|  2  | ***Taurus Fire.***  When a creature makes a saving throw against the Dread Fire Rocket, if the sphere has moved at least 10 feet this turn, the creature takes an additional 10 (3d6) fire damage and is knocked prone on a failed saving throw.

\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br><br><br>

|   |  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  3  | ***Dweomer Fire.*** Creatures making Constitution saving throws to maintain concentration on a spell as a result of damage from the Dread Fire Rocket have disadvantage on the saving throw. If a creature fails the saving throw, it takes an additional 2 (1d4) fire damage per level of the spell it was concentrating on.|

\pagebreakNum

## Ashsworn Elders
*Dwarven Clerics Serving Fiery Gods of Tyranny*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Relics <br>

Within the foreboding depths of the dark iron cathedrals, all manner of atrocious sacrifices, iniquitous spells, and blasphemous prayers are evoked. These rites are performed so that the dread gods of the world’s deepest recesses may be communed with, allowing the faithful to bargain for even the smallest portion of their unholy magics.

The wretched practitioners who dwell within these accursed halls are the Fel Dwarf Ashsworn. Within the Fel Dwarf empire, they are the ones who hoard the most foul rites and incantations, keeping the altars of their avaricious deities placated with meat, bone, and souls. The Ashsworn rank second in the Fel Dwarf hierarchy, standing just below the demon smiths. Though both orders clash constantly as they vie for power and status within Fel Dwarf society’s ruthless ranks.

\columnbreak

<br><br><br>

Unlike their demon smith brethren, the Ashsworn do not rely on blasphemous ingenuity to invent new weapons and spells. Instead, they beseech their hellish masters directly through dark hymns and blood rituals, pleading for raw might. That power becomes terrifyingly evident when these clerics leave their temples and march onto the fields of war. With a guttural cry and the raising of their onyx stone hammers, they draw forth the burning blood of the earth and hurl it at their foes, searing away skin and flesh in an agonizing death.

So dreadful are these conduits of destruction and suffering that even fellow Fel Dwarfs fear to fall before them. In their presence, warriors have been known to withstand wounds that would have slain them otherwise. Yet, just like the demon smiths, such malign practices come at a terrible cost. The magics of their heartless masters slowly turn the Ashsworn’s bodies to stone, trapping them eventually in a living prison of rock.


___

> - **Speed** 25 ft.

> ### Traits
>
> ***Aura of Fanaticism.*** The dwarf radiates an invisible protective aura in a 30-foot radius. The aura is inactive while the dwarf is Incapacitated. Whenever the dwarf or an ally within the aura takes damage of 15 or less that would reduce it to 0 hit points while it is Resolute*, it is reduced to 1 hit point instead.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Spellcasting.*** The dwarf is a spellcaster. Its spellcasting ability is Wisdom (spell save DC 15, +7 to hit with spell attacks). It knows the following spells: <br>
> **At Will:** *Guidance*, *Thaumaturgy*, *Fire Bolt* (3d10) <br>
> **3rd Level (3 Slots):** *Magmatic Stone*** <br>

>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf's turn, it becomes Resolute until the start of its next turn.
>
> ***Extract Power.*** The dwarf draws power, becoming Resolute and Empowered until the end of its next turn. While Empowered, whenever the dwarf hits with an attack, it deals an additional 10 (3d6) Fire damage. If the dwarf's Resolute condition ends before the end of its next turn, the power backfires; the dwarf is no longer Empowered and gains one level of Petrification. For each level of Petrification, its speed is reduced by 5 feet and its AC increases by 1. If the dwarf accumulates three or more levels of Petrification, it becomes Petrified until freed by a *Greater Restoration* spell cast using a 9th-level spell slot.
>

\pagebreakNum

### Fel Dwarf Ash Sworn Tactics

The primary goal of an Ashsworn is to secure a position where they can become Resolute, increasing the damage of their spell attacks and activating their Aura of Fanaticism, which bolsters nearby Fel Dwarfs.

Once established, the Ashsworn employs the following spells according to battlefield conditions:

**Magmatic Stone.**
Used when enemies are beyond the range of the Ashsworn's other offensive spells. The Ashsworn targets the front of an enemy formation whenever possible, increasing the likelihood that the storm created by the spell will pass through additional targets and hinder the enemy's advance.

**Bellows Breath.**
Bellows Breath is used as soon as two or more enemy creatures are within range. Friendly Fel Dwarfs will often position themselves to be affected by the breath as well, allowing them to benefit from its effects. This spell is particularly effective when combined with Shard Storm, as it enhances the damage dealt by subsequent attacks.

**Shard Storm.**
Shard Storm is typically cast after Bellows Breath whenever it can strike two or more enemies within range. The spell takes advantage of the bonuses granted by Bellows Breath, allowing the Ash Sworn to maximize its damage against clustered foes.

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
>  * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
>  * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
> 
> The condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

### New Spells

#### Bellows Breath
*5th-level Evocation (Artificer, Cleric, Paladin)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** Self (50-foot Cone)
- **Components:** V
- **Duration:** Instantaneous

You breathe out the fires of creation, reforging your allies and searing your foes. Each creature in a 40-foot Cone must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 3d10 Fire damage and is Tempered until the end of your next turn. While Tempered, the creature takes an extra 1d6 Fire damage whenever it is hit by an attack.

You may choose up to three creatures within the area to automatically succeed on this saving throw, taking no damage. You and those creatures are instead Heated until the end of your next turn. While Heated, whenever a creature hits another creature with an attack, it deals an extra 1d6 Fire damage on that hit.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** The initial damage increases by 1d10 for each slot level above 5th.

#### Magmatic Stone
*3rd-level Evocation (Sorcerer, Wizard)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** 100 feet
- **Components:** V, S, M (a bit of igneous rock)
- **Duration:** 1 round

You conjure a molten rock and hurl it at a creature within range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Bludgeoning damage. Whether the attack hits or misses, the rock explodes on impact, creating a 15-foot-radius sphere of whirling magma centered on the point of impact. Its area is Lightly Obscured and Difficult Terrain. The sphere spreads around corners and seeps through openings at least 5 feet in diameter.

At the start of your next turn, the sphere moves 30 feet in the direction you hurled it and dissipates. Any creature in the sphere when it first appears, and any creature whose space the sphere enters or passes through, must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 4d6 Fire damage. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** The Bludgeoning and Fire damage both increase by 1d6 for each slot level above 3rd.

\columnbreak

#### Shard Storm
*4th-level Evocation (Sorcerer, Wizard)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** 50 feet (50-foot Cone)
- **Components:** V, S
- **Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 minute
-  **Classes:**  Artificer

You send a wave of burning rock shards from your fingertips flying in a 50-foot Cone. Choose up to five creatures you can see in the cone. Make a ranged spell attack against each target. On hit, target takes 3d8 Piercing damage and a shard embeds itself in the creature. At the end of each of the creature's turns until the spell ends, it takes 2d8 Fire damage if the shard is still embedded.

**Removing the Shard.** A creature within reach can use an action to make a Wisdom (Medicine) check against your spell save DC to remove the shard. On a success, the shard is removed. On a failure, the shard is still removed, but the creature takes 1d8 Slashing damage.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** You may target one additional creature for each spell slot level above 4.

\pagebreakNum

## Bull Centaur Abominations
*Unholy Fusions of Dwarf, Bull, & Iron*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Grasslands, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>

Many daemonsmiths devise their own twisted methods to create bull centaurs when their numbers are too few to serve the needs of their malevolent plots. Yet the most ambitious among the fel artificers are never satisfied. They continue to subject these creatures to horrific experiments in pursuit of greater power.

The rare few that survive such brutal trials are known as abominations. Their hides are fused with metal, etched with hellish runes, and the blood of countless unholy creatures courses through their veins. 

\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br>

Infernal spearguns replace their hands—used to impale any who draw near or to launch burning projectiles at distant foes. Their hooves strike with such fury that they shatter the earth and ignite the very air, leaving rubble and broken bodies scattered about them.

### Bull Centaur Abomination Tactics
The abomination uses Cataclysmic Slam as soon as it can hit two or more targets. It prioritizes using Infernal Skewer against enemies who are Paralyzed or Frightened.

##### Bull Centaur Abominations Art Credit
* Burning Oil Well at Night - James Hamilton

>
> ***Overwhelming Stomp.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one prone or smaller creature. *Hit:* 32 (4d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage and the target must succeed on a *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 19. <br>
>

> *Failure by 5+:* It also can’t take reactions and must Dash away from the bull centaur by the safest route each turn.

> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone and cannot stand until the end of its next turn unless it spends all movement to make a DC 19 Constitution saving throw. On a success, it stands up.
>
> ***Cataclysmic Slam (1/Day).*** Each creature in a 30-foot cube originating from the bull centaur must make a *Strength or Dexterity Saving Throw (target’s choice):* DC 19. <br>
> *Failure:* 22 (4d10) thunder plus 22 (4d10) fire damage, and the target is knocked Prone. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is also Paralyzed until the end of the bull centaur’s next turn. <br>
> *Success:* Half damage only. <br>
> The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain if it's loose earth or stone.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
> ***Resolute Charge.*** At the start of its turn, bull centaur may move up to its speed in a straight line and becomes Resolute*.
>
> ### Reactions
> ***Retaliating Fire.*** *Trigger:* A creature 15–90 ft. away deals damage to the bull centaur. <br> *Response:* The bull centaur makes an Infernal Skewer attack against the attacking creature. The attack automatically misses if there is a hostile creature within 5 ft. of the bull centaur that is not Incapacitated.

### Bull Centaur Abomination Loot
Most of the valuable goods that can be looted from bull centaurs are the remains of the equipment they were using or valuables they were carrying or hiding nearby. Most dropped or hidden valuables can be found using **Intelligence (Investigation)** or **Wisdom (Perception)** checks. 

Alternatively, you can roll for the loot randomly using a flat modifier.

##### Bull Centaur Abomination Loot Table  (Avg. 1,250gp)


| Looting Check Result or 1d20 + 7 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-9  | 1 500gp Items |
|  10-14 | 1d4(2) 500gp Items | 
|  15-21 | 1 1,000gp Items  |  
|  22-25 | 1d4(2) 1,000gp Items |  
|  26+ |  1 2,000gp Items |  

#### Optional: Describing Looted Materials
If you’re not interested in the specifics, simply say the party collected materials worth the rolled gp value. If you want more detail, the tables below suggest materials for different gp values by creature. They also provide **Example Names** and **Quality Descriptors** for items worth **half** or **twice** the table's gp value.

For example, a 1,000gp Bone item from a bull centaur could be a Abominable Centaur Horn, a 500gp version might be a Damaged Abominable Centaur Horn, and a 2,000gp version could be an Enhanced Abominable Centaur Horn.

***When To Use Descriptions.*** You can describe these items during looting, when selling them, or when crafting, but it's best not to make players track long descriptive names. Only use as much detail as is needed when needed.

##### Bull Centaur Abomination Materials: 1,000gp 

|1d4 | Material | Example Name (Quality Descriptors) | 
|:--:|:---:|:-----------:|
| 1   | Bone|   Abominable Centaur Horn (Damaged/Enhanced) | 
| 2   | Jewelry | Fire Opal (Damaged/Flawless)|  
| 3-4   | Metal|  Abominable Centaur Plating (Damaged/Masterwork) |  

#### Optional: Magic Loot for Item Crafting
If you plan to use harvested loot for crafting magic items, you can try to thematically align the effects of the magic items being crafted with the creature's they're harvested from. In the case of bull centaurs, Fiendish effects would make the most sense. These could be magic item effects that steal the lives of their victims or conjure unholy magic.

***Magic Loot GP Values.*** The value of magic ingredients depends on your crafting system. Subtract their cost from the normal loot’s gp value. If the ingredient is worth significantly more, subtract it from the next treasure hoard instead.

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
> * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
> * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
The Resolute condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

\pagebreakNum

## Doom Cannons
A terrifying fusion of demon and war machine, the doom cannon stands as the pinnacle of fel dwarf siegecraft. This nightmarish engine is created by binding the soul of a powerful demon into brass and iron. The imprisoned spirit grants the cannon a horrifying semblance of life—groaning and shaking with unearthly hatred, yearning only for destruction.

These cursed weapons are overseen by sadistic engineers, typically fel dwarfs, who do not merely crew the doom cannon but rather guide it as one would a rabid beast. They feed the war machine freshly slain bodies, shoveling corpses into its rear chamber. Within the infernal mechanism, flesh and bone are swiftly liquefied, their souls extracted to fuel its chaotic flames. These unnatural fires erupt from the cannon’s maw,  scorching the battlefield and warping anything unfortunate enough to be caught in their wake.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

Among the deadliest instruments in the fel dwarfs’ arsenal, doom cannons are often sold to unholy warbands in exchange for vast hoards of slaves or plundered wealth. Tribes of fiend-worshipping marauders and orders of dark knights frequently employ them as artillery in their accursed crusades. However, those who wield these monstrous weapons must eventually pay the true price of their power.

Even with experienced handlers, the safeguards meant to contain the demon within inevitably fail. Many doom cannons have surged forward into enemy lines, abandoning their handlers in fits of uncontrollable bloodlust. At other times, the chaotic energies within grow unstable, causing deadly magical disruptions or leading to catastrophic explosions that consume all nearby—friend and foe alike. Some turn upon their own masters, scarring their minds with unspeakable horrors—or worse, drag them screaming into their fiery cores, where their souls remain trapped within the infernal machine.


___
___
> ## Doom Cannon
> *Huge fiend construct, chaotic evil*  
> ___
> - Armor Class 18 (natural armor)  
> - Hit Points 187 (15d12 + 90)  
> - Speed 40 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |22 (+6)|6 (-2)|22 (+6)|1 (-5)|1 (-5)|1 (-5)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Wisdom -1, Intelligence -1, Charisma -1  
> - **Damage Resistances** cold, lightning  
> - **Damage Immunities** poison, fire  
> - **Condition Immunities** charmed, exhaustion, frightened, paralyzed, petrified, poisoned  
> - **Senses** blindsight 60 ft., passive Perception 5  
> - **Languages** understands Abyssal but can't speak  
> - **Challenge** 10 (5,900 XP)   &emsp; Proficiency Bonus +4  
> ___
> 
> ***Bound Demon.*** The cannon is magically bound to three other creatures. At the start of each of the cannon's turns during combat, each of the bound creatures within 5 feet of the cannon must make a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw to maintain control of it. If none of the creatures succeed on the saving throw or no creatures make a saving throw, roll on the Doom Cannon Mishap Table (found at the end of the document).  
> 
> ***Consumes Corpse.*** The cannon or another creature can use its action to feed the corpse of a Medium or Small creature that died within the last 24 hours and is within 5 feet of the cannon into it. The creature’s corpse, along with all nonmagical items it is wearing or carrying, is destroyed, and its soul is trapped within the cannon until the cannon dies. While a creature’s soul is trapped, it cannot be resurrected.  
>
> ***Heavy Treads.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws and checks made against effects that would knock it prone or move it against its will. It must spend all of its movement to stand up and cannot squeeze through spaces.  
> 
> ***Immutable Form.*** The cannon is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.  
> 
> ***Magic Resistance.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.  
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Ram.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 25 (3d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage. If the cannon moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the attack, it deals an additional 19 (3d12) bludgeoning damage. If this attack kills a creature, the cannon can use its Consumes Corpse feature as a bonus action.  
> 
> ***Fire! (Recharges when the cannon consumes a corpse or after a long rest).*** The cannon fires a bolt of chaos flames at a point 30 to 600 feet away from itself. Each creature within 15 feet of that point must make a DC 18 Dexterity saving throw, taking 36 (8d8) fire damage on a failed save, or half as much on a success. This damage ignores fire resistance. If a creature fails the saving throw by 5 or more, it suffers a mutation* (Mutation Save DC 18).
>
> **New condition described at end of section* 



\pagebreakNum

### Ice-Forged Doom Cannon
In the cursed, frostbitten lands where malevolent forces hold sway, the fel dwarfs devised a chilling variant of the doom cannon: the ice-forged doom cannon. Unlike its fiery counterpart, this dreadful siege engine is crafted by subjugating the living nightmares that haunt the frozen wastes.

When fed a corpse, the engine grinds the body into crimson ice shards, transmuting the remains into its deadly ammunition.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

It then unleashes them in a merciless barrage of unholy frost. This accursed ammunition mercilessly drains the warmth and vitality from its victims, leaving only frozen husks behind.

Many savage barbarian tribes dwelling in these forsaken lands pay exorbitant sums to acquire such weapons, and survivors of an ice-forged doom cannon’s onslaught often find themselves swiftly overwhelmed by zealots of the dark gods.


___
___
> ## Ice-Forged Doom Cannon
> *Huge fiend construct, chaotic evil*  
> ___
> - Armor Class 18 (natural armor)  
> - Hit Points 187 (15d12 + 90)  
> - Speed 40 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |22 (+6)|6 (-2)|22 (+6)|1 (-5)|1 (-5)|1 (-5)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Wisdom -1, Intelligence -1, Charisma -1  
> - **Damage Resistances** fire, lightning  
> - **Damage Immunities** poison, cold  
> - **Condition Immunities** charmed, exhaustion, frightened, paralyzed, petrified, poisoned  
> - **Senses** blindsight 60 ft., passive Perception 5  
> - **Languages** understands Abyssal but can't speak  
> - **Challenge** 11 (7,200 XP)   &emsp; Proficiency Bonus +4

> ***Bound Demon.*** The cannon is magically bound to three other creatures. At the start of each of its turns during combat, each of the bound creatures within 5 feet of the cannon must make a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw to maintain control of it. If none of the creatures succeed on the saving throw or no creatures make a saving throw, roll on the Doom Cannon Mishap Table (found at the end of the document).  
> 
> ***Consumes Corpse.*** The cannon or another creature can use its action to feed the corpse of a Medium or Small creature that died within the last 24 hours and is within 5 feet of the cannon into it. The creature’s corpse, along with all nonmagical items it is wearing or carrying, is destroyed, and its soul is trapped within the cannon until the cannon dies. While a creature’s soul is trapped, it cannot be resurrected.  
> 
> ***Heavy Treads.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws and checks made against effects that would knock it prone or move it against its will. It must spend all of its movement to stand up and cannot squeeze through spaces.  
> 
>
> ***Immutable Form.*** The cannon is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.  
> 
> ***Magic Resistance.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Ram.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 25 (3d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage. If the cannon moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the attack, it deals an additional 19 (3d12) bludgeoning damage. If this attack kills a creature, the cannon can use its Consumes Corpse feature as a bonus action.  
> 
> ***Fire! (Recharges when the cannon consumes a corpse or after a long rest).*** The cannon fires three bolts of chilling ice at three different points within 30 feet of each other that are 30 to 600 feet away from itself. Each creature within 10 feet of a point must make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw, taking 36 (8d8) cold damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a success.  This damage ignores cold resistance. If a creature fails the saving throw by 5 or more, it suffers a level of exhaustion. A creature in the area of more than one chilling ice burst is affected only once.  
>


\pagebreakNum

### Doom Cannon of Damnation
The doom cannon of damnation is an even more powerful iteration of the doom cannon—an exceedingly rare and perilous creation, requiring the greatest mastery of dark artificers. Unlike its predecessors, this monstrous weapon does not merely rain fire or ice upon its enemies; instead, it unleashes torrents of soul-rending energy.

Those caught in its malignant blasts have their very essence wrenched from their bodies, their souls drawn into the cannon to fuel its devastating black magic. A single doom cannon of damnation can reap the souls of entire armies, trapping them within its iron frame to perpetuate its insatiable hunger.

\columnbreak

<br>

Yet, for all its devastating power, even the most ruthless warlords hesitate to wield such a weapon. Should the demon within ever break free, the consequences would be catastrophic. There are whispers of a battle where one such cannon escaped its handlers' control, feasting indiscriminately upon all combatants. When the dust settled, nothing remained of the armies that had clashed—only the doom cannon of damnation itself, rolling onward across the land, with the souls of every fallen warrior stowed within its depths.




___
___
> ## Doom Cannon of Damnation
> *Huge fiend construct, chaotic evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 18 (natural armor)  
> - **Hit Points** 187 (15d12 + 90)  
> - **Speed** 40 ft.

> 
> ***Bound Demon.*** The cannon is magically bound to three other creatures. At the start of each of its turns during combat, each of the bound creatures within 5 feet of the cannon must make a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw to maintain control of it. If none of the creatures succeed on the saving throw or no creatures make a saving throw, roll on the Doom Cannon Mishap Table (found at the end of the document). 
>
> ***Consumes Corpse.*** The cannon or another creature can use its action to feed the corpse of a Medium or Small creature that died within the last 24 hours and is within 5 feet of the cannon into it. The creature’s corpse, along with all nonmagical items it is wearing or carrying, is destroyed, and its soul is trapped within the cannon until the cannon dies. While a creature’s soul is trapped, it cannot be resurrected. 
>
> ***Heavy Treads.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws and checks made against effects that would knock it prone or move it against its will. It must spend all of its movement to stand up and cannot squeeze through spaces.  
> 
> ***Immutable Form.*** The cannon is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.  
> 
> ***Magic Resistance.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Ram.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target.  *Hit:* 25 (3d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage. If the target is a creature, its soul becomes linked to the cannon for 1 minute, until the cannon dies, or until a *remove curse* spell is cast on the creature. At the start of each of the cannon's turns, each creature linked to it within 100 feet takes 12 (3d8) necrotic damage. If this damage kills a creature, the cannon recharges its Fire ability, and the creature's soul is trapped within the cannon (as per its Consumes Corpse trait).
>
> If the cannon moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the attack, it deals an additional 19 (3d12) bludgeoning damage. If this damage kills a creature, the cannon can use its *Consumes Corpse* feature as a bonus action.  
> 
> ***Fire! (Recharges when the cannon consumes a corpse or after a long rest).*** The cannon fires a soul devouring energy blast at a point 30 to 600 feet away. Each creature within 15 feet of the target point must make a DC 18 Charisma saving throw, taking 36 (8d8) necrotic damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a success. This damage ignores necrotic resistance. If a creature fails the saving throw by 5 or more, it becomes linked to the cannon as described in the Ram attack.  
> 

\pagebreakNum




### Doom Cannon Mishap Table

| d6  | Mishap Name         | Mishap Effect |
|:----:|:------------------|:--------------|
| **1**  | Demonic Rush | The cannon must move toward the closest non-bound creature it can see and then use its action to make a slam attack against it if the creature is within range. If the creature is not within range, it takes the Dash action to move closer to the target. |
| **2**  | Fel Feedback | The cannon's energies backlash, disrupting the winds of magic. Until the end of its next turn, the cannon cannot use its Fire! ability, and any creature that attempts to cast a spell of 1st level or higher within 300 feet of the cannon must make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 3 (1d6) force damage per level of the spell, and the spell has no effect and is wasted. |
| **3**  | Vengeful Rampage | The cannon lashes out, using its action to make a slam attack against each creature within 5 feet. |
| **4**  | Pulse of Terror | The cannon's demonic essence instills terror in those around it. Each creature bound to the cannon within 30 feet becomes frightened of it until the end of the cannon's next turn. While frightened, affected creatures cannot take reactions and must use the Dash action on their turn, moving away from the cannon by the safest available route. |
| **5**  | Unstable Blast | The cannon's speed becomes 0 until the start of its next turn. Its Fire! ability recharges and must be used this turn. All creatures targeted by the blast have disadvantage on the saving throw. |
| **6**  | Free at Last! | The cannon explodes, destroying itself. Each creature within 30 feet suffers the effects of its Fire! ability as if they were in its blast radius and have disadvantage on the saving throw. |



\columnbreak

### Doom Cannon Tactics
Doom cannons are usually operated by Fel Dwarf engineers who keep them supplied with corpses. When well-fed, they stay back and bombard enemies from a distance.

If they run out of bodies, their handlers let them charge into enemy lines to refuel. When left on their own, doom cannons have no tactics, only an uncontrollable urge to destroy.

### New Condition: Mutated
When a creature is affected by this condition, it suffers one of the random mutations described in the Mutation Table below. A creature can't suffer from two instances of the same mutation at once.

Unless an ability states otherwise, a creature suffering from one or more mutations can make a Charisma saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effects of all mutations on a success. The save DC is equal to the highest save DC among any effects that inflicted it with its current mutations.

##### Mutation Table
| d6  | Mutation |  Creature Effect  |
|:---:|:---------:| :---------:|
|  1  |  Wild Spasms | The creature can't take reactions, and on each of its turns it must choose whether it gets a move, an action, or a bonus action; it gets only one of the three. |
|  2  | Degeneration  | The creature's form becomes brittle.  While a creature is brittle, any attack that hits the creature is a critical hit.|
|  3  | Twisted Arms |  The creature can no longer wield weapons or cast spells with somatic components and it has disadvantage on all weapon attack rolls.|
|  4  | Inverted Eyes | The creature is blinded.|
|  5  | Gelatinous Legs | The creature falls prone and can't stand up.|
|  6  | Regeneration | The creature regains 10 hit points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 hit point.|

\pagebreakNum

## Fel Dwarf Dreadnaughts
*Possessed Dwarves Entombed Within Iron War Suits*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>

Among the many unholy practices embedded in fel dwarf society, few are as perilous or profane as the binding and enslavement of demonic entities. Within blasphemous forges, forge-workers perform dread rites under the direction of demon-smiths—dark rituals that summon malevolent spirits from other worlds and bind them into metal, infusing weapons and war machines with diabolical power.

These procedures are extremely hazardous. The summoned fiends viciously resist their captors, struggling to break free and wreak vengeance on those who would subjugate them. When such incantations go awry, the demon may possess any flesh-and-blood participant in the summoning—even the demon-smith themselves. The possessed are plunged into agony and hatred, driven into berserk rages by the invading essence. They lash out at everything nearby, attempting to slaughter all within reach before being subdued or consumed by the torment wracking their bodies.

To salvage such disasters, demon-smiths act swiftly. The possessed victim is restrained and forcibly grafted into a hastily forged exoskeleton of iron. Enchantments are laid upon the frame to tether the struggling soul within and suppress the demon’s full control. But the dwarf is not freed from suffering. Instead, they are forever bound to their armor—a living furnace fueled by the demon trapped within their flesh, gnawing at the edges of their mind.

\columnbreak

<br>

These malformed fusions are known as Dreadnoughts—living engines of destruction. No longer truly dwarfs, they serve the demon-smiths without hesitation, living only to spread the agony they themselves endure. They lash out at any foolish enough to oppose the fel dwarf empire. Their forged forms can withstand immense punishment; only those cunning or fortunate enough to pierce the armor and strike the dwarf buried within can end their miserable existence.

### Molten Dreadnaughts
The torment endured by molten dreadnoughts is used to heat their blackened metal bodies until they burn red-hot, boiling the air around them and igniting any who draw their ire. Their metal gauntlets sear flesh on contact, and built-in tanks release torrents of boiling chemicals to unleash tides of searing death.

### Ebon Dreadnaughts
Clad in sleek black steel, ebon dreadnoughts are suffused with entities of malice that draw strength from the shadows. Used as assassins by the elite of fel dwarf society, they mold umbral spears from surrounding darkness, piercing foes with weapons that leave no visible mark but inflict unspeakable misery. Once the deed is done, they melt back into the gloom from whence they came.

\pagebreakNum

### Tormentor Dreadnaughts
Dreadnoughts with demons of death bound within are horrific indeed. These entities devise macabre weapons that keep their adversaries barely alive, preserving the faintest glimmer of life so they can be dragged back to the demon-smiths and enslaved—forced to live out their existence in endless agony.

<br>

\columnbreak

### Earthbane Dreadnaughts

The most terrible of demons are trapped within Hulking Dreadnoughts—massive horrors of sinister power. These brutes are equipped with hellish drills and claws, each capable of tearing through granite and sundering fortifications.

To add to their lethality, their enormous claw can unlatch from their armored frames, springing forward on thick chains. Should a claw strike a solid target, it clamps shut with crushing force and is swiftly reeled back toward the hulking warrior.

The drill is an even greater instrument of devastation. By piercing it into the ground, an earthbane dreadnought sends shockwaves rippling through the earth, causing it to scream and shatter. The ground erupts skyward in violent upheaval, crashing down in a cataclysmic wave that buries and batters any caught within.

<br>

> ___

> - **Hit Points** 204 (24d10+72)
> - **Speed** 40 ft., burrow 20 ft.

> - **Skills** Athletics +10, Intimidation +5  
> - **Immunities** Fire, Poison; Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned  
> - **Senses** Darkvision 30 ft., Passive Perception 10  
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal  
> - **Challenge** 11 (7,200 XP)      **Proficiency Bonus** +4  
> ___
> ### Traits
>
> ***Siege Monster.*** The dreadnaught deals double damage to objects and structures.
>
> ***Tunneler.*** The dreadnaught can burrow through solid rock at half its burrowing speed and leaves a 5-foot-wide, 8-foot-high tunnel in its wake.
>
> ***Overdrive.*** When the dreadnaught fails a saving throw that required its action to make or fails a saving
throw at the beginning or end of a turn, it can choose to succeed instead. If it does so, it takes force damage equal to 3d10 plus the amount by which it failed the save.
>
> ***Weak Point.*** Whenever a creature has advantage on an attack roll against the dreadnaught, it can choose to roll only one die instead of two to aim for the dreadnaught's weak point. If the attack hits, the dreadnaught has vulnerability to bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing damage from that attack. The attack still counts as having advantage for the purposes of other abilities, such as a rogue’s Sneak Attack.
>
> ### Actions
> ***Multiattack.*** The dreadnaught makes one Grip Claw attack or uses its Crushing Vise, then makes one Fel Drill attack. It can replace any of these attacks with a Swat .
>
> ***Swat.*** *Strength or Dexterity Saving Throw (target's choice):* DC 18, one target with in 10 feet 0f the dreadnaught.<Br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 22 (4d10) bludgeoning damage, is knocked prone, and is pushed up to 30 feet in a straight line away from the Dreadnaught.  <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target also cannot stand until the end of its next turn unless it spends all its movement to make a DC 18 Constitution saving throw. On a success, it stands up.
>
> ***Grip Claw.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 30 ft., one target. *Hit:* 28 (4d10 + 6) bludgeoning damage. If the target is Large or smaller, it is Grappled (escape DC 18) and pulled up to 30 feet straight toward the dreadnaught. Until this Grapple ends, the target is Restrained, and the dreadnaught can’t use Grip Claw on another target.
>
> ***Crushing Vise.*** *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 18, one creature Grappled by the dreadnaught. <br> 
> *Failure:* 36 (8d8) bludgeoning damage, and the target becomes brittle until the end of its next turn. While brittle, any attack that hits the target is a critical hit. <br> 
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is also paralyzed until the end of its next turn.  <br>
> *Success:* Half damage only.
>
> ***Fel Drill.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 28 (4d10 + 6) piercing damage, and the target must make a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 18 (disadvantage if the attack was a critical hit). <br> 
> *Failure:* The dreadnaught automatically hits the target with another Fel Drill attack as part of this attack. The dreadnaught can only make one extra attack this way. <br> 
> *Failure by 5+:* The extra attack also deals an extra 16 (3d10) piercing damage on hit.
>
> ***Upheaval (3/Day).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 18, Each creature in a 20-foot-wide, 40-foot-high, 60-foot-long line on the ground originating from the dreadnaught.  
> *Failure:* 19 (3d12) bludgeoning damage, and the target is knocked Prone.  
> *Failure by 5+:* If the target is prone on earth or stone, it becomes buried as it sinks into the ground. A buried creature is Restrained, Blinded, has total cover, and is suffocating. Any creature, including the buried one, can use its action to free the buried creature by succeeding on a DC 18 Strength check. This check has advantage if the creature making it has a burrow speed.  <br>
> *Success:* Half damage.  <br>
> *Success or Failure:* The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain if it is made of earth or stone.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of its turn, the dreadnaught becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
> 
> **Condition described at the end of the section*

</span>

\pagebreakNum

### Fel Dwarf Dreadnaught Tactics

All dreadnoughts rely heavily on maintaining their Resolute Stance to prevent enemies from gaining advantage and striking their weak points.

#### Molten Dreadnought Tactics

Molten dreadnoughts seek to ignite as many targets as possible during combat. They use Naphtha Spray whenever they can hit two or more enemies (or other molten dreadnoughts) simultaneously. If no enemies are within melee range, they will also use Naphtha Spray to maintain the benefits of their Resolute Stance.

#### Ebon Dreadnought Tactics

Ebon dreadnoughts strike from the shadows, targeting creatures that are already in darkness while keeping their distance. If surrounded by melee attackers, they will use Shadow Step to reposition themselves and regain the advantage.

#### Tormentor Dreadnought Tactics

Tormentor dreadnoughts excel as defenders, aiming to minimize enemy movement and incoming damage. They open combat by using Torturous Containment against the creature with the lowest physical stats and the most health. They then employ Umbreal Binding on two different targets to further restrict enemy movement.

#### Earthbane Dreadnought Tactics

Earthbane dreadnoughts focus on isolating and crushing weaker ranged attackers. They use Grip Claw to pull low-Strength ranged enemies into melee, then immediately strike with their Fel Drill. If the target remains grappled on the dreadnought’s next turn, it uses Crushing Vise followed by another attack with the Fel Drill.

If the Dreadnought fails to grapple a target, or if it can hit three or more enemies at once, it uses Upheaval instead of Fel Drill.

The Dreadnought uses Swat to push away frontline warriors who obstruct its path to priority targets.

### Fel Dwarf Dreadnaught Loot
Most of the valuable goods that can be looted from fel dwarf dreadnaughts come from what can be salvaged from their chassis. Salvaged material can be gathered using an Intelligence (Arcana), Intelligence (Smith’s Tolls), or Intelligence (Tinker’s Tools) check.

Alternatively, you can roll for the loot randomly using a flat modifier.

##### Molten Dreadnaught Loot (Avg. 44gp)

| Looting Check Result <br> or 1d20 + 5 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-9  | 2d4(5) 5gp Items (25gp) |
|  10-14 | 1d6(3) 10gp Items (30gp) | 
|  15-21 | 2d5(5) 10gp Items (50gp) |  
|  22-23 | 1d6(3) 20gp Items (60gp)  |  
|  24+ |  2d4(5) 20gp Items (100gp) |

#### Optional: Describing Looted Materials
If you’re not interested in the specifics, simply say the party collected materials worth the rolled gp value. If you want more detail, the tables below suggest materials for different gp values by creature. They also provide **Example Names** and **Quality Descriptors** for items worth **half** or **twice** the table's gp value.

For example, a 10gp Metal item from a dreadnaught could be Dreadnaught Scrap, a 50gp version might be a Worn Dreadnaught Scrap, and a 200gp version could be a Masterwork Dreadnaught Scrap.

***When To Use Descriptions.*** You can describe these items during looting, when selling them, or when crafting, but it's best not to make players track long descriptive names. Only use as much detail as is needed when needed.

|1d4 | Material | Example Name (Quality Descriptors) | 
|:--:|:---:|:-----------:|

#### Optional: Magic Loot for Item Crafting
If you plan to use these creatures’ loot for crafting magic items, consult the Magic Themes table for thematic suggestions. The **Magic Theme** column helps align the crafted item’s effects with the creature’s nature, while the **Example Descriptor** suggests how to describe the loot's magical quality. Magic loot can span one or multiple themes.
 
For example, Fiendish magic item effects might drain the life of their victims or conjure unholy magic, while Shadow effects might create darkness or utilize necrotic magic. 

***Random Magic Ingredients.***
To introduce randomness to how often magic loot is found, roll a percentile die along with the normal loot roll to see if magic loot is found. There's a suggested probability at the top of the table.

##### Fel Dwarf Dreadnaught Magic Themes 
**Suggested Chance of Finding Magic Loot: 75%**
|1d4 | Magic Theme | Example Descriptor | 
|:--:|:---:|:-----------:|
| 1-2 <br>  | Fiendish| Demon-Touched| 
| 3-4 (Molten)  | Fire | Ever-Burning|  
| 3-4 (Ebon)  | Shadow  | Light-Devouring| 
| 3-4 (Tormentor)  | Fiendish  | Demon-Touched| 
| 3-4 (Earthbane)  | Earth  | Earthen| 

***Magic Loot GP Values.*** The value of magic ingredients depends on your crafting system. Subtract their cost from the normal loot’s gp value. If the ingredient is worth significantly more, subtract it from the next treasure hoard instead.

\columnbreak


### New Condition: Resolute
The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
 * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
 * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
 * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
 
The condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.
 
#### New Condition: Burning
 The creature has been set ablaze by something that continues to damage it each round until it is extinguished. The default source described here is fire, but other sources could deal different types of damage, such as acid.
* **Default Damage:** A burning creature takes 1d4 fire damage at the end of each of its turns unless the source that inflicted the condition states otherwise.
* **Ending the Condition:** As an action, the burning creature can extinguish the fire by dropping prone and rolling on the ground. The fire also goes out if it is doused, submerged, or suffocated. For example, another creature could use an action to put out the flames with their cloak or by pouring water on them.
* **Multiple Sources of Burning:** If a burning creature would gain this condition again from another source, the new burning effect only replaces the old if it deals more damage each round. Otherwise, it is ignored.

<br>

> ##### Notes on 2024 Burning Rules
> If you're using the rules for burning described in the 2024 edition of D&D, these are the changes that were made in this document:
> * **Sources of Burning Don't Stack.** If a burning creature would gain this condition again from another source, the new burning effect only replaces the old if it deals more damage each round. Otherwise, it is ignored.
> * **Damage At End of Turn.** Burning damage occurs at the end of a creature's turn rather than the beginning. This gives the creature the chance to try to end the condition before taking the damage, thus giving players more agency when they start burning.

## Gordak Knifetwister
*A Fel Goblin Boss Who Uses Luck, Underhanded Tactics, & Disposable Minions to Come Out On Top*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>

Fel goblins rarely live long lives. The constant struggle beneath their infernal dwarf masters, combined with their own treacherous instincts, ensures that most of these vile grunts meet their end by a crude shiv driven deep into the spine or a well-placed arrow to the back of the head.

Gordak Knifetwister, however, has survived through a continuous chain of unfortunate miracles, persisting long past the expiration date of his disposable kin. Filled with vitriol, cunning, and uncanny good fortune, Gordak is able to sidestep even the most expertly placed attacks and retaliate with his Blade of Malice, a cruel weapon capable of bypassing even the strongest defenses to strike where a target is most vulnerable.

He carries a pair of cursed dice said to aid him in escaping death’s grasp, whether by granting him the strength to pry himself free from a ferocious beast’s maw or sharpening his wits enough to see through some dreadful illusion.

Gordak has risen far above any fel goblin before him, amassing a large band of wolf riders and becoming one of the most formidable and sought-after groups of hirelings within the fel dwarf empire. Leading from atop his ragged and irritable mount, Thrall Trap’a, Gordak and his riders are able to cheat death itself and secure entire workforces of laborers for the highest bidder.

### Gordak's Lair

Gordak never stays in one place for long. Constantly on the move, he roams in search of his next bounty, or his next victim. When he lingers in an area for any length of time, he and his flunkies throw together some ramshackle camp for shelter. Other times, the cruel band of marauders can be found lurking within the dark towers and forsaken facilities of the fel dwarves.

Wherever Gordak travels, he always seems to have a fresh supply of minions willing to take upon themselves the grisly fate he truly deserves.

When fighting within their lair, Gordak's Challenge Rating (CR) increases by 2.

#### Lair Action
When either Gordak or Thrall Trap’a rolls initiative, their lair awakens to aid them. Until combat ends or both die, the lair has an Intelligence of 16 and can act of its own accord, always acting in the best interest of both. While active in this way, the lair is aware of the location of all creatures within it. On initiative count 1 (losing initiative ties), the lair chooses an unoccupied space at the edge of its boundaries. A Gordak Boyz (stat block on the page after next) appears in the space and remains until destroyed. It acts on initiative count 0 (losing initiative ties).

\columnbreak

<br><br><br>



### Gordak's Tactics
Gordak focuses on a single low-AC target to build Luck Points and apply weakness against his attacks. He constantly repositions himself so that no two enemies are within 5 feet of him, allowing his Slippery ability to remain active.

Gordak rolls his Crooked Dice whenever:
* He is affected by a condition or effect that allows repeated saving throws, and he does not currently have advantage on those saves
* An enemy is repeatedly forcing him to make a saving throw without advantage.

#### Gordak & Thrall Trap’a Tactics
When fighting alongside Thrall Trap’a, Trap’a uses his bite attack first against the nearest target within range, preferably one that Gordak is not currently attacking.

* If the bite misses, Trap’a uses Menacing Growl on Gordak’s target to frighten it and provoke attacks of opportunity from Gordak.
* If the bite hits, Trap’a flings the target away unless it is Gordak’s chosen target. If it is Gordak’s target, Trap’a keeps the creature Grappled instead.

#### Gordak's Layer
When they appear, Gordak's boys will first hit a target with their burning arrows, then dash into melee combat to absorb damage for Gordak.  

<br>

> ##### New Condition: Burning
> The creature has been set ablaze by something that continues to damage it each round until it is extinguished. The default source described here is fire, but other sources could deal different types of damage, such as acid.
> * **Default Damage:** A burning creature takes 1d4 fire damage at the end of each of its turns unless the source that inflicted the condition states otherwise.
> * **Ending the Condition:** As an action, the burning creature can extinguish the fire by dropping prone and rolling on the ground. The fire also goes out if it is doused, submerged, or suffocated. For example, another creature could use an action to put out the flames with their cloak or by pouring water on them.
> * **Multiple Sources of Burning:** If a burning creature would gain this condition again from another source, the new burning effect only replaces the old if it deals more damage each round. Otherwise, it is ignored.



\pagebreakNum

### Epic Battle: Fang and Blade
You can pair Gordak with is steed Thrall Trap'a to make him an even greater threat. Together, they should be treated as having a combined Challenge Rating (CR) of 13.

\columnbreak

> ##### Trained Beasts
>
> Trained beasts acquire skills over the years, similar to how players gain experience in a class. This means spells and abilities, such as *polymorph* or a druid's Wild Shape, cannot transform a creature into a trained beast.

>
> ### Traits
>
> ***Back Stabber.*** Gordak has advantage on opportunity attacks. Creatures provoke opportunity attacks from Gordak even if they take the Disengage action before leaving his reach, though the attack made against a creature this way has disadvantage instead of advantage.
>
> ***Lucky Git.*** Every time Gordak succeeds on a saving throw or hits with an attack roll during combat, he gains a luck point up to a maximum of 10. He loses all luck points at the end of combat or when he fails a saving throw or misses with an attack roll.
>
> ***Reactive.*** Gordak can take one reaction on every turn in combat.
>
> ***Slippery.*** Attacks against Gordak have disadvantage and deal half damage to him unless Gordak has a speed of 0 or there are two or more hostile creatures within 5 feet of him.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Blade of Malice.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) Piercing damage plus 2 (1d4) Piercing damage for each luck point he has. Gordak deals an additional 7 (2d6) Piercing damage if the attack was made using his reaction. If the target is a creature, it gains one level of Vulnerability to Gordak, up to a maximum of 10.
> 
>For each level of Vulnerability to Gordak a creature has, the critical hit threshold for Gordak's attacks made against that target is lowered by 1. At the end of the creature's turn, if it hasn't received a level of Vulnerability to Gordak since its last turn, it loses all levels. 
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Crooked Dice.*** Gordak rolls 1d6 and gains the corresponding perk tied to an ability score for the next minute, or until he gains another perk. While he has the perk, he has advantage on checks and saving throws made with the chosen ability. Additionally, when Gordak makes a saving throw using that ability against a spell or effect that allows a save to take only half damage, he takes no damage on a success and only half damage on a failure. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**1. Cunning.** Intelligence. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**2. Quick.** Dexterity. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**3. Tough.** Constitution. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**4. Brawny.** Strength. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**5. Smooth.** Charisma. <br>
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**6. Intuitive.** Wisdom. <br>
>
> ### Monstrous Actions
>
> Gordak gains one additional action in combat on Initiative Count 10. He can take any of the following actions if he is not Incapacitated. He can also take Tenacious Will even if Incapacitated.
>
> ***Stick 'Em (Initiative Count 10).*** Gordak or a creature he is mounted on moves up to 15 feet without provoking opportunity attacks and then Gordak makes a Blade of Malice attack.
>
> ***Leg It (Initiative Count 10).*** Gordak or a creature he is mounted on moves up to 30 feet without provoking opportunity attacks.
>
> ***Tenacious Will.*** If Gordak is subject to an ongoing effect that allows him to make a saving throw as an action or at the start or end of his turn, he can immediately make that save with advantage, ending the effect on a success.
>
> ### Reactions
>
> ***Better Them Than Me.*** *Trigger:* A creature attacks Gordak. <br>
> *Response:* Gordak chooses an ally within 5 feet of him and within range of the attack to become the target of the attack. If no other creatures are within range, the reaction is wasted. If the target is reduced to 0 hit points this way, Gordak gains a luck point.

> - **Speed** 50 ft.

> ### Traits
>
> ***Pack Tactics.*** Thrall Trap'a has advantage on an attack roll against a creature if at least one of Thrall Trap'a's allies is within 5 feet of the creature and the ally isn't Incapacitated.
>
> ***Slippery Mount.*** While Gorduz is mounted on Thrall Trap'a, attacks against Thrall Trap'a have disadvantage and deal half damage unless it has a speed of 0 or there are two or more hostile creatures within 5 feet of it.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Bite.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 16 (3d8 + 3) Piercing damage. If the target is a creature, it must make a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 14. <br>
> 
> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone and is Grappled if it is Large or smaller (escape DC 14). While Grappled, Thrall Trap'a can't use its Bite attack against another target or use Menacing Growl.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Menacing Growl.*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 14, a creatures within 60 feet of Thrall Trap.
> *Failure:* The target becomes Frightened of Thrall Trap'a. The Frightened target can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success, and is immune to Menacing Growl for the next hour. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* While Frightened in this way, the target can't take Bonus Actions or reactions and must use its action on its turn to Dash and move away from Thrall Trap'a by the safest route on each of its turns unless there is nowhere to move.
>
> ***Fling.*** Thrall Trap'a chooses one Medium creature it is holding or Grappling and throws it up to 15 feet away. If the target hits a solid surface, it takes 6 (1d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage and lands Prone. If the thrown target would hit another creature, that creature must make a *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 14. <br>
> *Failure:* The creature takes 6 (1d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage and is knocked Prone if it is Huge or smaller. <br>
> *Success:* The creature dodges out of the way, takes no damage, and is not knocked Prone.
>
> ### Reactions
>
> ***Sick 'Em.*** *Trigger:* Gorduz takes the Attack action while mounted on Thrall Trap'a as a controlled mount. <br>
> *Response:* Thrall Trap'a makes a Bite attack against a target within 5 feet.

\pagebreakNum


## Fel Dwarf Harridans
*Spiteful Old Dwarf Women Serving as <br> Berserkers & Bodyguards*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>

Within the merciless society of the Fel Dwarfs, females are afforded little care or respect. Their status is only above the countless throngs of slaves gathered by their dark empire. Their sole purpose is to bear children, with all their material needs met by thralls and fel goblins. Companionship among the male dwarves is viewed as weakness. As a result, Fel Dwarf women live miserable, sequestered lives, kept far from the plots and schemes of their male counterparts.

This isolation lasts until they reach an age where they are no longer fertile and thus hold no further value to their kind. At that point, most are discarded. Many are sacrificed on altars by their own sons to the malevolent gods of their race, while others are sent to labor alongside the other captives, toiling until they break and fade away with the rest of the broken souls.

Some of these aged women, however, choose a violent end. They unleash the bitterness accumulated over a lifetime of subjugation upon the enemies of the Fel Dwarfs, proving their worth to the evil gods even in their final years. Taking up great iron mauls, they become known as Harridans.

On the battlefield, these aged battle-maidens wear little to no armor and display a fearlessness unmatched by any other of their kind. As a Harridan draws nearer to death, her ferocity only grows. Many Demon Smiths and Ash Sworn Elders are accompanied by one or two of these savage warriors, for the Harridans are all too ready to intercept any attack made against high-ranking officers without hesitation, and they deliver swift vengeance against those who would dare strike at such figures.

\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br><br>

Even after suffering brutal treatment at the hands of the very society fashioned by these vile spellcasters, Harridans remain unquestioningly devoted to the empire, so much so that many burn out their own tongues to ensure they can never question their orders.

Such is the dark tragedy of these berserkers.

<br>

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
>  * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
>  * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
> 
> The condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

\pagebreakNum

### Fel Dwarf Viragos
The most effective Harridans, those who prove exceptional at safeguarding the hierarchy of the dark clergy and the lords who rule the city-states, may be elevated to the rank of Virago. Only those with the quickest reflexes and sharpest instincts have any chance of selection. Most earn this honor by saving their master from death, intercepting a well-placed arrow or bullet.

Such a step in rank comes with even greater trials. Demon Smiths subject these candidates to forbidden alchemy and sacrilegious surgeries to further heighten their reflexes. Those who survive emerge as spry, deadly creatures, gliding across the battlefield, effortlessly sidestepping projectiles only to pin down foes and deny them any chance of escape from their wrath.

#### Harridan & Virago Tactics
Harridans act as bodyguards, staying close to spellcasters and ranged artillery. They use their Redirect ability to absorb as much damage as possible until they become Bloodied, at which point they rush forward to attack.

Viragos excel at blocking and intercepting ranged attacks, aggressively pursuing enemy ranged units to prevent them from disengaging.

### Fel Dwarf Hell Brides
Even among the oppressed female population, the craving for power and domination still burns. While most females who defy their assigned place suffer punishments so vile they wish for death, a few take the risk. These ambitious women seek out forbidden dark magic and attempt to reach beyond the veil to make pacts with entities that might free them from servitude.

Most fail, either discovered and brutally disciplined or consumed by the treacherous forces they try to command. But a rare few, wicked and sadistic enough, manage to gain the favor of a devil or malevolent god. These scheming matrons ascend and become Hell Brides.

Hell Brides can imbue themselves with unholy magic, granting them the power to split the earth asunder, snap an orc’s neck with their bare hands, and shrug off weapon blows as if their skin were stone. Though hated by the rest of Fel Dwarf society, with Demon Smiths and Ash Sworn constantly plotting their downfall, the raw power and divine favor Hell Brides command allow them to carve out status within the clergy and avoid open conflict.

With the influence they consolidate, Hell Brides become fearsome warlords, all too eager to inflict their accumulated misery upon their foes. They cause the earth to buckle and break beneath their enemies while pushing their own troops beyond their limits, absorbing their pain and forcing them to fight on even as death tries to claim them.

Like all Fel Dwarf magic, the infernal power comes at a terrible cost. The sorcery slowly turns the Hell Bride’s body to stone. Yet their spite is so great that they force the petrified flesh to burn molten. When petrification finally takes full hold, they do not become lifeless statues like their male counterparts. Instead, the hateful inferno within causes their calcified bodies to erupt in a catastrophic explosion of molten rock. When a Hell Bride senses her end approaching, there is no greater sadistic joy than dragging as many souls as possible with her in a final wave of searing stone.

#### Fel Dwarf Hell Bride Tactics
Fel Dwarf Hell Brides cast Earth Rupture and use Absorb Pain until they are Bloodied, at which point they will use Molten Infusion and start attacking targets. They will move into the largest group of enemies once they reach 2 levels of Petrification.
