#### New Spell: Earthen Rupture
*4th-level Evocation (Druid)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** Self (60-foot Line)
- **Components:** V, S
- **Duration:** Instantaneous
- **Classes:**  Druid

You rend asunder the earth in a 5-foot-wide line up to 60 feet long. Creatures touching the ground in the area must make a Strength saving throw. On a failed save, they take 4d6 Bludgeoning damage and fall Prone. On a successful save, they take half as much damage and don't fall Prone. Large or larger objects and structures in the area automatically fail the save and take twice as much damage. 

The ground in the area becomes Difficult Terrain until cleared. Each 5-foot-square portion requires at least 1 minute to clear by hand.

**Earthen Pillar.** A pillar of stone bursts from the ground centered on the far end of the line. The pillar has a diameter of 5 feet and a height of up to 40 feet. The ground where the pillar appears must be wide enough for its diameter, otherwise, it doesn't appear. The pillar has AC equal to your spell save DC and 25 hit points. When reduced to 0 hit points, the pillar crumbles into rubble, creating an area of Difficult Terrain with a 10-foot radius that lasts until cleared.

**Creatures Caught On The Pillar.** If the pillar is created beneath a creature, that creature must succeed on a Dexterity saving throw or be lifted by the pillar. A creature can choose to fail this save.

If the pillar is prevented from reaching its full height by a ceiling or other obstacle, a creature on the pillar takes 3d6 Bludgeoning damage and is Restrained, pinched between the pillar and the obstacle. The Restrained creature can use its action to make a Strength or Dexterity check (its choice) against your spell save DC. On a success, the creature is no longer Restrained and must either move off the pillar or fall from it.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** The damage of both effects increases by 1d6 for each slot level above 4th.

> - **Resistances** Fire, Poison; damage from ranged attacks while the dwarf is Resolute
> - **Gear** Maul
> - **Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 15
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal
> - **Challenge** 7 (2,900 XP)   &emsp;&emsp;&emsp;&emsp;   **Proficiency Bonus** +3
> ___
>
> ### Traits
>
> ***Vexed.*** While the dwarf is Bloodied, it can move even while Resolute* and has Immunity to being Charmed and Frightened.
>
> ***Aura of Fanaticism.*** The dwarf radiates an invisible protective aura in a 30-foot radius. The aura is inactive while the dwarf is Incapacitated. Whenever the dwarf or an ally within the aura takes damage of 15 or less that would reduce it to 0 hit points while it is Resolute, it is reduced to 1 hit point instead.
>
> ***Molten Infusion.*** At the start of the dwarf's turn, it can choose to gain 30 temporary hit points that last for one minute. If it does, it gains one level of Petrification, its speed is reduced by 5 feet, and its AC increases by 1. If the dwarf accumulates three or more levels of Petrification, it turns to molten rock and explodes, dying. *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 16, each creature in a 20-foot Emanation originating from the dwarf. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 14 (4d6) Fire damage and 14 (4d6) Bludgeoning damage. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Impeding Maul.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit (with advantage if it is an opportunity attack), reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 19 (4d6 + 5) Bludgeoning damage, plus 11 (2d10) Fire damage for each level of Petrification the dwarf has. Regardless, the target also can't take the Disengage or Dodge action on its next turn. If the damage reduces the target to 0 hit points, that creature is Stunned for the next minute.
>
> ***Spellcasting.*** The dwarf is a spellcaster. Its spellcasting ability is Charisma (spell save DC 16, +8 to hit with spell attacks). It knows the following spells: <br>
> **4th Level (3 Slots):** *Earthen Rupture*** <br>
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf's turn, it becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
>
> ### Reactions
>
> ***Absorb Pain.*** *Trigger:* A creature the dwarf can see within 30 feet takes damage. <br>
> *Response:* The dwarf magically takes half the damage instead of that creature taking the full damage. This feature doesn't transfer any other effects that might accompany the damage, and this damage can't be reduced in any other way.
>

\pagebreakNum



## Infernal Defenders
Infernal defenders are cruel guardians fashioned by fel dwarf artificers using unholy methods similar to those employed in creating infernal destroyers. A lesser demon is bound into a body forged of metal and stone. These forms vary in shape, ranging from steel hounds with snapping jaws to brass bulls with searing horns, or vaguely humanoid figures twisted from obsidian.

Though these creations burn with the hatred of their imprisoned demons, they remain unwaveringly loyal to their masters, unleashing onslaughts of terrible fire upon their enemies. On command, the demon can channel its suppressed fury into another creature, allowing the recipient to deliver unnaturally brutal strikes at the cost of their own body, which is torn apart by the demon’s rage.

### Infernal Defender Tactics
The following tips can help you run infernal defenders in combat more effectively. In combat the infernal defender:
* Stays near its creator as a pseudo-protector and supporter.
* Utilizes Rebuke Attack to defend its allies.
* Employs Strain ability when commanded; it's most effective when used on targets:
    * Capable of dealing at least 20 damage with a single attack. (e.g., Deathscreecher Artillerist)
    * Possessing powers associated with their strikes (e.g., Doomquake Battle Smith.)

<br>

##### Infernal Defenders Art Credit
Kilauea - Ogura Yonesuke

\columnbreak

<br>

### Infernal Defender Variants

The type of demon bound to the construct can vary. You can apply one of the following abilities to the infernal defender to represent this, increasing its challenge rating (CR) by 1.

##### Infernal Defender Variants
| d3  | Ability |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Forge-Bound Demon.***  Creatures holding a metal object have disadvantage on saving throws against the Defender’s Rebuke attack. Additionally, if a creature fails a saving throw against the Rebuke Attack, a metal object of the Defender’s choice the creature is holding becomes red-hot for the next minute. A creature holding a object heated this way has disadvantage on attack rolls and ability checks unless it is immune to fire damage.|
|  2  | ***Shadow-Bound Demon.***  The defender now deals psychic damage instead of fire damage and is immune to psychic damage instead of fire damage. Its Rebuke Attack forces a creature to make a Wisdom saving throw instead of a Dexterity saving throw. Additionally, if a creature fails the save, it is blinded, in addition to taking damage. A creature can repeat the saving throw at the start of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success.|
|  3  | **Hunger-Bound Demon.** The defender now deals necrotic instead of fire damage and is immune to necrotic damage instead of fire damage. Its Rebuke Attack forces a creature to make a Constitution saving throw instead of a Dexterity saving throw. Additionally, the Defender heals a creature of it's choice within 5 feet of it a number of hit points equal to the damage dealt by its Rebuke Attack. |

## Magma Dragons
*Cataclysmic Harbingers of World-Shattering Destruction*<br>
**Habitat:** Volcanic; **Treasure:** Any <br>
Magma dragons are among the most powerful creatures to stalk the mortal planes. They are among the mightiest and most destructive of all draconic species, possessing power surpassed only by the gods themselves. These colossal behemoths make their abodes within volcanoes and vast magma-filled caverns that lie deep beneath the earth's surface, where they slumber for centuries atop their treasure hoards.

The treasure hoards of magma dragons are the envy of all other dragons, for the immense wealth of precious metals and rare minerals they gather from the world's depths is so vast that even the greatest human vaults of riches are mere trinkets by comparison.

When magma dragons finally awaken from their long slumber and soar forth from their torrid domains, the world shudders at their passing. The cataclysmic power they unleash is capable of disfiguring entire landscapes and reducing forests to ash within moments. Nor do magma dragons take any sorrow in the breaking of the world; rather, they revel in their ability to shatter all around them with absolute impunity. Viewing all other living things as little more than insects to be swept aside, the only things that give such tyrants pause are creatures or objects capable of withstanding the brutal force of their might. Such defiance awakens a malicious wrath, and a magma dragon will stop at nothing to assert its dominance and grind its opposition into rubble.

Nearly nothing can stand against such living forces of destruction. Those who have heard the tales of dragons, their torrents of flame and steel-rending claws, and still choose to bear arms against such legendary beasts are hopelessly naïve to the danger they face. To stand before a magma dragon is to stand before a living catastrophe.

Sweltering heat and toxic fumes pour endlessly from the dragon's body, sapping the life from all who venture too close. Its mighty wings beat with such force that they unleash blinding streams of ash that sting the eyes and crackle with lightning. Its immense bulk causes the earth to split and tremble, while thunderous shockwaves crush anything caught within their devastating wake. Claws sharper than obsidian cleave through the strongest metals and solid stone alike, tearing apart even the most heavily armored and resilient foes.

Greatest of all, however, is its breath. A magma dragon can exhale a torrent of annihilation: a cataclysmic flood of lava, lightning, fire, and toxic ash that surges across the land, sealing the doom of all in its path. The oldest of these dragons are said to be capable of engulfing an entire village with a single bellow.

##### Magma Dragons Art Credit
* An erupting volcano by night - David Humbert de Superville

\columnbreak



> ##### New Condition: Fatigue
> The creature experiences debilitating weariness that can accumulate in levels. Each time a creature gains the Fatigue condition, its Fatigue Level increases by 1. If the level reaches 6, the creature dies. Fatigue Levels have the following effects on a creature:
> * **d20 Rolls:** When a creature makes a d20 Test, subtract its Fatigue Level from the d20 roll.
> * **Spell Save DCs:** Subtract a creature's Fatigue Level from the Spell Save DC of any spell it casts.
> * **Speed** A creature's speed is reduced by a number of feet equal to 5 x its Fatigue level. This can't lower its speed below 5 feet.
> 
> **Ending the Condition:** Completing a Long Rest reduces a creature's Fatigue Level by 1.
> 
> **Fatigue vs. Exhaustion:** Fatigue is an alternative mechanic to the Exhaustion condition, and any ability that removes levels of Exhaustion or grants immunity to it does the same for Fatigue. 

<br>

### Magma Dragon Tactics
#### Opening Turn
The Magma Dragon begins combat by using its Breath Weapon, positioning it to hit as many targets as possible. It only uses Draconic Resistance if the effect causes it to be unable to attack effectively.

It prioritizes creatures with low mobility or limited ability to reposition, allowing it to stack Molten Wounds on the same targets over multiple rounds.

##### Initiative Count 21
The Magma Dragon uses the ability that can affect the greatest number of targets.
If multiple abilities would hit the same number of targets, it chooses in the following order:
* Ash and Lightning if it can hit at least one target with 2 or more Molten Wounds.
* Molten Snare if there is at least one additional target with low Dexterity that can be caught in the effect.
* Otherwise, use whichever available ability deals the most damage or provides the greatest battlefield control.

##### Initiative Count 1
The Magma Dragon uses Shattering Quake unless:
* It cannot hit two or more targets, or
* There is a target with 3 or more Molten Wounds that has not gained a Molten Wound this turn. In that case, the dragon prioritizes applying another Molten Wound to that target instead.

>

>
> ***Molten Snare (Initiative Count 21).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 15, one creature within 60 feet of the dragon that it can see. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 7 (2d6) Fire damage and is Restrained by hardening rock. A creature can use an action to make a DC 15 Strength (Athletics) check, freeing itself or a creature within reach from the rock on a success. The rock has AC 15 and 10 hit points, and is immune to Fire, Poison, and Psychic damage. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage only.
>
> ***Pounce (Initiative Count 1).*** The dragon moves up to half its speed and makes one Rend attack.
>
>
>
> ***Tenacious Will.*** If the dragon is subject to an effect that allows a saving throw at the start or end of its turn, it can immediately make that saving throw with advantage, ending the effect on a success. This action can be taken even while Incapacitated.

> ___
> - **Skills** Intimidation +13, Perception +14
> - **Resistances** Lightning, Poison, Thunder
> - **Immunities** Fire
> - **Senses** Blindsight 60 ft., Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 24
> - **Languages** Common, Draconic
> - **Challenge** 19 (22,000 XP)   &emsp;&emsp;&ensp;   **Proficiency Bonus** +6
> ___
>
> ### Traits
>
> ***Aura of Sweltering Heat.*** The dragon emits an aura of heat in a 15-foot emanation from itself. Each creature not immune to Fire that is within the emanation at the end of the dragon's turn must make a *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 21. <br>
> *Failure:* The creature takes 7 (2d6) Fire damage and is Poisoned until the end of its next turn. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The creature also gains one level of Fatigue*. <br>
> If the dragon takes 15 or more Cold damage, this trait doesn't function until the end of the dragon's next turn.
>
> ***Draconic Power.*** The dragon has 3 charges. It gains a charge at the start of each of its turns, up to a maximum of 3 charges.
>
> ***Draconic Resistance.*** If the dragon fails a saving throw, it can spend a charge and choose to succeed instead.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Multiattack.*** The dragon makes two Rend attacks.
>
> ***Rend.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +15 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 20 (2d10 + 9) Slashing damage. If the target is a creature, it receives a Molten Wound. For each Molten Wound a creature has, its AC is lowered by 1 and it suffers a -1 penalty to all Constitution saving throws. This effect can't reduce a creature's AC below 1. At the end of the creature's turn, if it hasn't received a Molten Wound since its last turn, it loses all Molten Wounds.
>
> ***Volcanic Breath (3 Charges).*** The dragon exhales volcanic devastation in a 90-foot cone. *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 22, each creature in the cone. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 27 (6d8) Poison damage, 27 (6d8) Fire damage, and 27 (6d8) Lightning damage, and gains one level of Fatigue*. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target gains two levels of Fatigue instead. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage only.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Dread Presence.*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature of the dragon's choice within 120 feet of it that is aware of it. <br>
> *Failure:* The target is Frightened for 1 minute. While Frightened, the creature can't cast spells other than cantrips. It can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* While Frightened, the creature can't take reactions or Bonus Actions and must use the Dash action on its turn to move away from the dragon by the safest route. <br>
> *Success:* The target is immune to the dragon's Dread Presence for the next hour.
>
> ### Monstrous Actions
>
> The dragon gains two additional actions in combat, each performed on its own initiative count, separate from the dragon's: one performed on initiative count 21 and the other on initiative count 1. On each initiative count (losing ties), the dragon chooses one of the available actions for that count if it is not Incapacitated. It can replace any action with Tenacious Will, even if Incapacitated.
>
> ***Ash and Lightning (Initiative Count 21).*** *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature in an 80-foot-long, 15-foot-wide line originating from the dragon. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 14 (4d6) Lightning damage, is pushed back 30 feet, loses concentration, and is Blinded until Initiative Count 21 of the next round. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target is Paralyzed until Initiative Count 21 of the next round. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage only.
>
> ***Molten Snare (Initiative Count 21).*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature in a 15-foot-radius Sphere centered on a point within 120 feet of the dragon. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 14 (4d6) Fire damage and is Restrained by hardening rock. A creature can use an action to make a DC 21 Strength (Athletics) check, freeing itself or a creature within reach from the rock on a success. The rock has AC 15 and 20 hit points, and is immune to Fire, Poison, and Psychic damage. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target also receives a Molten Wound. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage only.
>
> ***Pounce (Initiative Count 1).*** The dragon moves up to half its speed and makes one Rend attack.
>
> ***Shattering Quake (Initiative Count 1).*** *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 21, each creature in a 25-foot Cube originating from the dragon. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 14 (4d6) Thunder damage and is knocked Prone. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target can't stand up until the end of its next turn. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage only.
>
> ***Tenacious Will.*** If the dragon is subject to an effect that allows a saving throw at the start or end of its turn, it can immediately make that saving throw with advantage, ending the effect on a success. This action can be taken even while Incapacitated.
> 
> **New condition described at beginning of section*

\pagebreakNum

## Shah'tar, The Grand Executioner
*Most Dreaded of All Bull Centaurs*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Grasslands, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>
In a desolate land where ash lies thick upon the ground, and clouds of black smoke conceal the earth from the idle gaze of the heavens, there winds a river of broken peoples—gathered from many lands, chained one to another like pebbles heaped by the will of crueler hands. At their flanks strode the dwarfs, grim and gleeful, whose whips cracked like thunder, driving the wretched forward without word or mercy.

Through dust-choked vistas they marched, mile upon mournful mile, until from the haze arose a temple—tall and black, and sudden as a robber springing from shadow.

Its gates, wrought of iron and rimmed with carvings of winged beasts most foul, yawned wide like the mouth of some ravenous demon, eager to devour the throng that marched toward it. Within, the slaves beheld vast halls, their walls of obsidian lit only by ancient runes that burned—not with fire, but with the hate of the hands that carved them. And in that place there rose a statue, hewn from ebon marble, in the image of a dark and hateful god. Its gaze fell ever downward, filled with scorn for those who crawled at its feet.

There, upon a brass altar beneath that blasphemous effigy, stood a creature made in the likeness of the god that loomed above. Its lower half was that of a mighty bull, shod in hooves like charred stumps of trees, and its hide gleamed like beaten steel. From the waist upward it was a dwarf, though of hue ashen and lifeless, and over its face hung a mask of braided chains, through which gleamed the souls of demons, ever whispering of sin. In its gnarled hands it bore an axe of dreadful beauty, forged of gold and red iron, and faintly the spirits of slaughter could be heard chanting a chorus in praise of the ruin it had wrought.

And those who had come thus far beheld this vision, and terror took them. What little hope had lingered fled from their hearts, and they became still—for no lashing of whips could drive them onward, not to that forsaken god nor to his monstrous servant. But the demons in chains awakened. The scent of fear hung thick in the air, and from the mask they curled forth like serpents of smoke, winding about the slaves and rending flesh and spirit alike, seeking anguish to feast upon before the end.

Then the slaves fell to their knees, and the great beast came near. It raised its axe, and the spirits of slaughter howled. The temple floor shook with the laughter of its hateful god.

Thither go all the rivers of slaves that wander the desolate land, and the end of every river is the same—a sea of blood upon the black temple’s floor.

And upon that sea drift severed heads, their eyes wide with dread, still gazing upward at the monster who had made such a sea, who yet stood unmoving. For he who is forged to wield the blade of death is Shah'tar, The Grand Executioner of the dark god—and his labor shall not cease.


\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br>

### Shah'tar's Lair
Shah'tar makes his lair within a great temple of the dark god he serves.

When fighting within his lair, Shah'tar's Challenge Rating (CR) increases by 2.

#### Sacrificial Circle
When Shah'tar rolls initiative, his lair awakens to aid him. Until combat ends or Shah'tar dies, the lair has an Intelligence of 14 and can act of its own accord, always acting in the best interest of Shah'tar. While active in this way, the lair is aware of the location of all creatures within it. On initiative count 20 (losing initiative ties), the lair creates a 10-foot-radius, 30-foot-tall cylinder of hellish energy centered on a point on the ground within the lair that lasts until initiative count 20 of the next turn. It causes the following effects:

* A creature in the lair that is outside of the cylinder and attempts to cast a spell of 1st level or higher must make a *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 19.<br>
*Failure:* The spell dissipates with no effect, and the action, bonus action, or reaction used to cast it is wasted. If that spell was cast with a spell slot, the slot isn’t expended.<br>
*Success:* The creature casts the spell as normal.
* A creature inside the cylinder that casts a spell of 1st level or higher takes 3 (1d6) necrotic damage per level of the spell. This damage can't be reduced in anyway.
* A creature other than Shah'tar in the lair that is outside of the cylinder subtracts 1d6 from all its damage rolls.
* Whenever a creature other than Shah'tar ends its turn in the cylinder, it takes 10 (3d6) fire damage.


#### Regional Effects
The area where Shah'tar dwells is corrupted by his presence, creating one or more of the following effects everywhere within 5 miles of his lair:

* Whenever a creature hits with an attack that rolls a 20 on the attack roll, that target takes an extra 4d6 damage.
* Whenever a creature rolls a 1 on a saving throw, demonic spirits appear and Restrain it until they are destroyed (AC 15; HP 20; immunity to Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing, Fire, Necrotic, and Poison damage). While Restrained, the creature can't teleport and can't cast spells other than cantrips.
* Any attack that hits creature with 0 hitpoints is a critical hit if the attacker is within 5 feet of the creature.

These effects gradually dissipate over 1d10 days after Shah'tar's death.

> ### Traits
>
> ***Marked for Death.*** At the start of his turn, Shah'tar can mark one creature he can see for death. For 1 hour or until he uses this action again, whenever he hits that creature with an attack, he deals an extra 9 (2d8) slashing damage.
>
> ### Actions
>
>
> ***Multiattack.*** Shah'tar makes two attacks, each with a different weapon.
>
> ***Dazing Gore.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 15 (2d8 + 6) Piercing damage. If Shah'tar moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the hit, the target must make a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 19.<br>
> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is also Dazed* until the end of its next turn. <br>
>
> ***Pinning Stomp.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one Prone creature or one creature two sizes smaller. *Hit:* 26 (3d12 + 6) Bludgeoning damage. The target is Grappled (escape DC 20), Restrained while Grappled, and Shah'tar cannot use Pinning Stomp again or move until this Grapple ends.
>
> ***Hamstringing Greataxe.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 19 (2d12 + 6) Slashing damage. If the target is Prone, it suffers a crippling wound. 
> 
> While wounded this way, the target can't stand up. The wounded creature, or a creature within 5 feet of it, can use an action to make a DC 19 Wisdom (Medicine) check, ending the effect on a success. The effect also ends if the creature regains 15 or more hit points.
>
> ***Execute.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one creature that is marked by the Marked for Death trait, Grappled, Prone, or Restrained. *Hit:* 100 slashing damage, or 50 slashing damage if the attack didn’t have advantage. If the target is reduced to 0 hit points, it is decapitated and dies unless it can survive without its head.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Resolute Charge.*** At the start of his turn, Shah'tar may move up to his speed in a straight line and become Resolute* until the start of his next turn.
>
> ### Monstrous Actions
>
> Shah'tar gains two additional actions in combat: Proclamation of Doom and Death Toll. Each action can be performed on its own initiative count, separate from Shah'tar's. On the action's initiative count (losing ties), Shah'tar can take the action if he is not incapacitated. He can replace either action with Tenacious Will, even if incapacitated.
>
> ***Proclamation of Doom (Initiative Count 11).*** Shah'tar summons demonic spirits of doom, targeting two creatures within 60 feet of his choice that were not targeted by this ability during the last round. Those creature's must make a *Charisma Saving Throw:* DC 19. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes 13 (3d8) necrotic damage, is moved 10 feet in any direction of Shah'tar's choice, and is Restrained by the demonic spirits until they are destroyed (AC 15; HP 20; immunity to Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing, Fire, Necrotic, and Poison damage). While Restrained, the creature can't teleport and can't cast spells other than cantrips. <br>
> *Failure by 5 or More:* The target is also knocked Prone, and any spells of 4th level or lower affecting the creature end. <br>
> *Success:* The target takes half damage and suffers no other effects.
>
> ***Death Toll (Initiative Count 1).*** Shah'tar can move up to 25 feet and makes a Greataxe or Execute attack during this movement.
>
> ***Tenacious Will.*** If Shah'tar is subject to an ongoing effect that allows him to make a saving throw as an action or at the start or end of his turn, he can immediately make that save with advantage, ending the effect on a success.
>
> **New condition described at the end of the section*

### Epic Battle: The Mass Executioner
If you wish to make Shah'tar into a true threat worthy of providing the final battle to a campaign, you can double his maximum hit points, bringing the total up to 588 hit points, and give him the Mass Execution trait, described below; this increases his CR to 21.

***Mass Execution (Recharges after a short or long rest).*** If Shah'tar becomes Boodied (294 HP), his Marked for Death trait is immediately triggered, targeting any number of creatures within 90 feet of him. He then replaces his Death Toll action with Massacre, described below.

***Massacre. (Initiative Count 1).*** Shah'tar can move up to 50 feet. During the movement, he may either make one Hamstringing Greataxe or three Execute attacks; each attack must be against a different target.
 
### Shah'tar Tactics
Shah'tar's primary goal in combat is to use his Execution attack to finish off weakened foes.

#### Marked for Death 

Shah'tar designates a preferred target for execution based on one or more of the following factors:

* **Restrained or Prone:** The target is already restrained and/or prone.
* **Reachability:** Shah'tar favors targets he can reach in a straight line, enabling use of his Resolute Charge action.
* **Low Armor Class:** If multiple valid targets are available, he prioritizes the one with the lowest AC, especially if it's Restrained.

\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br><br>

#### Normal Turn
On a typical turn, Shah'tar prepares his prey using a combination of:
1. Dazing Gore to knock the target prone
2. Pinning Stomp to restrain them
3. and if the target is already restrained, a combination of Dazing Gore followed by Execution.


#### Proclamation of Doom
This ability targets creatures that are either Marked for Death or have low Charisma saving throws.


#### Death Toll
Shah'tar always attempts an Execution attack if a target is eligible. If no valid execution targets are available, he instead uses his Hamstringing Greataxe to attack a Prone target, if possible.


#### Sacrificial Circle
The Sacrificial Circle cylinder is placed over the largest cluster of Restrained or Prone creatures within range.

\pagebreakNum



### Bull Centaur Executioners
Within the iron temples of the fel dwarves, countless sacrifices are made to appease their avaricious gods. At the heart of the grandest of these macabre rituals stands the hulking figure of a bull centaur executioner. These beasts wear black chain veils over their faces and wield ornately carved axes forged from unholy volcanic rock.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

With deadly precision, they pin down struggling victims beneath their hooves and, with a single decisive swing, separate head from neck. Their strikes are so exact and powerful that even full-grown ogres have been cleanly decapitated in this manner.

#### Bull Centaur Executioner Tactics
Executioners mark creatures with low Strength scores and will relentlessly focus on executing that target for the remainder of the combat.

If reduced to 0 hit points, the target is decapitated and dies unless it can survive without its head.

> ***Resolute Charge.*** At the start of its turn, bull centaur may move up to its speed in a straight line and become Resolute*.

### Bull Centaur Loot
Most of the valuable goods that can be looted from bull centaurs are the remains of the equipment they were using or valuables they were carrying or hiding nearby. Most dropped or hidden valuables can be found using **Intelligence (Investigation)** or **Wisdom (Perception)** checks. 

Alternatively, you can roll for the loot randomly using a flat modifier.


##### Bull Centaur Executioner Loot Table <br> (Avg. 82gp)

| Looting Check Result or 1d20 + 5 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-6  | 2d6(7) 5gp Items |
|  7-10 | 2d4(5) 10gp Items | 
|  11-17 | 2d6(7) 10gp Items  |  
|  18-22 | 2d4(5) 20gp Items |  
|  23+ |  2d6(7) 20gp Items |  

##### Shah'tar, The Executioner Loot Table <br> (Avg. 2,550gp)


| Looting Check Result or 1d20 + 7 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-9  | 1 500gp Item |
|  10-13 | 1 1,000gp Item | 
|  14-20 | 1d4(2) 1,000gp Items  |  
|  21-25 | 1d4(2) 2,000gp Items |  
|  26+ |  1d6(3) 2,000gp Items |  

### Optional: Describing Looted Materials
If you’re not interested in the specifics, simply say the party collected materials worth the rolled gp value. If you want more detail, the tables below suggest materials for different gp values by creature. They also provide **Example Names** and **Quality Descriptors** for items worth **half** or **twice** the table's gp value.

For example, a 10gp Bone item from a bull centaur could be a Bull Centaur Horn, a 5gp version might be a Young Bull Centaur Horn, and a 20gp version could be an Elite Bull Centaur Horn.

***When To Use Descriptions.*** You can describe these items during looting, when selling them, or when crafting, but it's best not to make players track long descriptive names. Only use as much detail as is needed when needed.

#### Optional: Magic Loot for Item Crafting
If you plan to use harvested loot for crafting magic items, you can try to thematically align the effects of the magic items being crafted with the creature's they're harvested from. In the case of bull centaurs, Fiendish effects would make the most sense. These could be magic item effects that steal the lives of their victims or conjure unholy magic.

***Magic Loot GP Values.*** The value of magic ingredients depends on your crafting system. Subtract their cost from the normal loot’s gp value. If the ingredient is worth significantly more, subtract it from the next treasure hoard instead.

<br>

> ##### New Condition: Dazed 
> A dazed creature can’t take reactions and on its turn, it must choose whether it gets a move, an action, or a bonus action; it gets only one of the three.

<br>

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
> * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
> * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
The Resolute condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

<br>

#### Shah'tar, The Grand Executioner Art Credit
* Caravane Dans Le Desert - Jean-Léon Gérôme

## Siege Giants
*Tormented Behemoths Wielding Grafted Weaponry*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark,  Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>
Few creatures are as legendary for their tumultuous and unruly nature as giants. Their immense size and strength allow them to indulge in reckless behavior without fear of repercussions. A giant lives only to roam, driven by the endless need for vast quantities of food to sate its voracious appetite and enough barrels of ale and mead to overcome its resilient fortitude. As they pursue their gluttonous desires, they leave behind broken landscapes and ruined villages.

The empires of the fel dwarves have witnessed these living weapons and sought to bend such power to their will, as is the nature of the fel dwarf. Many giants have been captured by the infernal enclaves, either through cunning trickery or overwhelming military force. These captives are dragged to massive foundries, where they undergo a torturous transformation. Dense metal plates studded with thick, protruding spikes are grafted onto the giant’s leathery skin. The brute’s meaty hands are torn off and replaced with enormous weapons—hooked blades capable of tearing down castle walls and pickaxes the size of oxen. These tortured engines of war are known as Siege Giants.

When battle looms, the towering iron behemoths stumble forth to carry out their master's cruel bidding. Their tormented minds and heavy grafted metal exteriors make them unpredictable and hazardous, wildly swinging their colossal weapons and pulverizing foundations wherever their rampage takes them. The brutal momentum of their iron-clad legs crushes stone as they stomp across the battlefield, oblivious to friend or foe.

### Siege Giant Tactics
Siege giants are dim-witted, tormented creatures that instinctively lumber toward the largest enemy or the source of their pain, attempting to destroy it if left without guidance.

However, when controlled by their fel dwarf masters, they are directed to unleash their area-of-effect attacks on clusters of enemies while reserving their single-target strikes for foes with weak saving throws against the attack's secondary effects.


\columnbreak

### Siege Giant Loot
Most of the valuable goods that can be looted from siege giants are the remains of the equipment that was grafted onto them. These items could be found or identified using **Intelligence (Investigation)** or **Wisdom (Perception)** checks, or they could be harvested from the giant's armaments using an Intelligence (Blacksmith's Tools) check.


Alternatively, you can roll for the loot randomly using a flat modifier.

##### Siege Giant Loot Table  (Avg. 3,525gp)


| Looting Check Result or 1d20 + 7 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-9  | 1d4(2) 500gp Items |
|  10-12 | 1d6(3) 500gp Items | 
|  13-19 | 1d4(2) 1,000gp Items  |  
|  20-24 | 1d4(2) 2,000gp Items |  
|  25+ |  2d4(5) 2,000gp Items |

If you’re not interested in the specifics, simply say the party collected materials worth the rolled gp value. If you want more detail, the tables below suggest materials for different gp values by creature. They also provide **Example Names** and **Quality Descriptors** for items worth **half** or **twice** the table's gp value.

For example, a 1,000gp Metal item from a siege giant could be Siege Giant Plating, a 500gp version might be Damaged Siege Giant Plating, and a 2,000gp version could be a Masterwork Siege Giant Plating.

***When To Use Descriptions.*** You can describe these items during looting, when selling them, or when crafting, but it's best not to make players track long descriptive names. Only use as much detail as is needed when needed.

In the case of siege giants, Fiendish effects would make the most sense. These could be magic item effects that steal the lives of their victims or conjure unholy magic.

***Magic Loot GP Values.*** The value of magic ingredients depends on your crafting system. Subtract their cost from the normal loot’s gp value. If the ingredient is worth significantly more, subtract it from the next treasure hoard instead.****

> ### Traits
>
>
> ***Siege Armor.*** Ranged attacks made against the giant from more than 15 feet away from it treat the giant as though it has an AC of 24.
>
> ***Implacable.*** The giant has advantage on saving throws against being Charmed, Frightened, Stunned, Paralyzed, or Incapacitated.
>
> ***Siege Monster.*** The giant deals double damage to objects and structures.
>
> ***Spiked-Covered Armor.*** When the giant falls prone for the first time each turn, it takes 23 (3d10 + 7) bludgeoning damage. Each other enemy creature within a 5-foot radius emanating from the giant must make a *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 20.<br>
> *Failure:* 23 (3d10 + 7) piercing damage.<br>
> *Success:* No damage.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Writhing Attack.*** The siege giant performs two of the following actions, chosen at random. If it has 100 hit points or fewer, it performs three actions instead. It can take these actions at any point during its turn, in any order:
>
> ***1. Yell and Bawl.***  *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 21, Each other enemy creature in a 120-foot radius emanating from the giant. <br>
> *Failure:* The creature is Frightened of the giant and repeats the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success. While Frightened, it can't cast spells other then cantrips.<br>
> *Failure by 5+:* While the creature is Frightened, it can’t take reactions and must take the Dash action on each of its turns and move away from the giant by the safest available route.
>
> ***2. Ripping Blades.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +13 to hit, reach 15 ft., one target.
> *Hit:* 45 (7d10 + 7) piercing damage, and the target must make a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 21. <br>
> *Failure:* The target takes an extra 10 (3d6) piercing damage. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target suffers a rending wound. While wounded this way, the target has disadvantage on Strength, Dexterity, and Constitution saving throws, checks, and attack rolls, and its speed is halved. The wounded creature, or a creature within 5 feet of it, can use an action to make a DC 20 Wisdom (Medicine) check, ending the effect on a success. The effect also ends if the creature regains 15 or more hit points.
>
> ***3. Stomp and Smash.*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 20, Each other creature in a 15-foot radius emanating from the giant. <br>
> *Failure:* 23 (3d10 + 7) bludgeoning damage. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is also knocked prone.
>
> ***4. Brutal Bash.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +13 to hit, reach 15 ft., one target.
> *Hit:* 45 (7d10 + 7) bludgeoning damage, and the target must make a *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 21. <br>
> *Failure:* The target is Incapacitated until the start of the giant’s next turn. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is Stunned until the end of the giant’s next turn.
>
> ***5. Leg Crusher.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +13 to hit, reach 15 ft., one target.
> *Hit:* 45 (7d10 + 7) bludgeoning damage, and the target must make a *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 21. <br>
> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone and can’t stand up unless it uses all of its movement. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target’s speed is 0 until the end of its next turn.
>
> ***6. Sweeping Blades.*** *Dexterity Saving Throw:* DC 21,  Each other creature in a 15-foot radius emanating from the giant. <br>
> *Failure:* 23 (3d10 + 7) slashing damage. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target also has disadvantage on attack rolls until the start of the giant’s next turn.

## Tyrant Engines
*Demonic Constructs Wielding Cruel Maces & Fiery Cannons*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, <br> Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>
 
Tyrant engines are insidious automatons brought to life by fel dwarf artificers through binding the very contemptuous thoughts of their dark patrons within the brass and iron frames of these inventions. Born from such hatred, these machines possess an innate urge to inflict pain and crush hope wherever their heavy metal frames tread. Even their makers have little control over their actions when they encounter a foe filled with courage, for such resolve is something they long to snuff out.

Their hulking metallic bodies are adorned with sculpted bull heads and possess the strength to send foes flying through the air, broken and limp. Even if such an impact does not outright kill an adversary, the tyrant engine carries a variety of murderous implements with which to finish its quarry.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

The greatest chance one has when facing such a manufactured avatar lies in the machine's unstable design. The animus that drives it also slowly consumes its internal workings. Should the artificial demon sustain significant damage, its frame can catastrophically fail as the malignant essence within breaks free and tears through the rest of the construct's body.

### Tyrant Engine - Bane Mace
Tyrant engines animated by the dark will to dominate and control are armed with immense maces. However, the construct's eyes are the feature one must be most wary of. These robotic warriors bear two blazing gems that burn with baleful power. When those hexstones become fixed upon a living soul, they sap away all determination, leaving the victim unable to raise their weapon against the foul spawn, or even freezing them completely in place. Only for the blackened mace of the tyrant engine come crashing through their rigid body.
___
___

> *Large Construct Fiend, Party, Lawful Evil*

> - **Hit Points** 228 (24d10 + 96)
> - **Speed** 40 ft.

> - **Immunities** Fire, Poison; Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned
> - **Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 9
> - **Languages** Understands Infernal but can't speak
> - **Challenge** 10 (5,900 XP)   &emsp;&emsp;&emsp;   **Proficiency Bonus** +4
> ___
>
> ### Traits
>
> ***Burning Bright.*** At the start of the engine's turn, if it has 160 or fewer hit points and took an action on its last turn, it gains a level of Instability and takes 20 Force damage for each level of Instability it has. It removes a level of Instability at the end of any turn if it gained 20 or more hit points since its last turn.
>
> ***Fire Absorption.*** Whenever the engine is subjected to Fire damage from a source other than itself, it takes no damage and instead regains a number of hit points equal to the Fire damage dealt.
>
> ***Immutable Form.*** The engine is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.
>
> ### Actions
>
> ***Multiattack.*** The engine makes two attacks. If the engine has two levels of Instability, it can only make one attack.
>
> ***Gore.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +9 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 32 (5d10 + 5) Piercing damage. If the engine moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the hit, the target must succeed on a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 17. <br>
> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone and pushed up to 20 feet away.
>
> ***Crushing Bane Mace.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +9 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 27 (4d10 + 5) Bludgeoning damage plus 16 (3d10) Fire damage. If the target is Prone, it takes an additional 11 (2d10) Bludgeoning damage and can't stand until the end of its next turn unless it spends all its movement to make a *Constitution Saving Throw:* DC 17. On a success, it stands up.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
>
> ***Dominating Glare.*** *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 17, one creature the engine can see within 60 feet. <br>
> *Failure:* The target is Charmed until the end of the engine's next turn. While Charmed, the target has disadvantage on Strength and Dexterity based d20 tests. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The target is Paralyzed while Charmed. <br>
> *Success:* The target is immune to this engine's Dominating Glare for 1 hour.

</span>

\pagebreakNum



### Tyrant Engine - Immolation Cannons
Tyrant engines driven by an overwhelming urge to destroy are fitted with massive cannons in place of their arms. Acting as living artillery, they unleash torrents of hellish fire that bathe the battlefield in flame. Such constructs become relentlessly fixated upon specific targets once battle is joined, stopping at nothing until their chosen victim has suffered an agonizing death and been reduced to nothing more than cinders.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

#### Tyrant Engine - Immolation Cannons Tactics
The tyrant engine marks a target that is surrounded by allies, then begins bombarding that target with its Immolation Cannons as it advances. It fights especially well alongside allies that are resistant or immune to fire damage, or that absorb fire damage. These allies should help by knocking the marked target prone.

The engine does not suffer disadvantage on this attack from the target being Prone and instead has advantage. <br>
> *Failure:* The creature takes 10 (3d6) Fire damage, or 21 (6d6) Fire damage if the target is marked. <br>
> *Failure by 10+:* The creature starts Burning*, taking 10 (4d4) Fire damage at the end of each of its turns until the condition ends.
> 
> **New condition described on next page*

</span>

\pagebreakNum




> ##### New Condition: Burning
> The creature has been set ablaze by something that continues to damage it each round until it is extinguished. The default source described here is fire, but other sources could deal different types of damage, such as acid.
> * **Default Damage:** A burning creature takes 1d4 fire damage at the end of each of its turns unless the source that inflicted the condition states otherwise.
> * **Ending the Condition:** As an action, the burning creature can extinguish the fire by dropping prone and rolling on the ground. The fire also goes out if it is doused, submerged, or suffocated. For example, another creature could use an action to put out the flames with their cloak or by pouring water on them.
> * **Multiple Sources of Burning:** If a burning creature would gain this condition again from another source, the new burning effect only replaces the old if it deals more damage each round. Otherwise, it is ignored.

<br>

> ##### New Condition: Obsessed 
> The creature becomes fixated on a creature or object, which becomes a source of its obsession. Being obsessed has the following effects on a creature:
> * While the creature can see a source of its obsession, it can't target any creature or object with an attack, spell, or ability except for a source of its obsession.
> * While the creature can see a source of its obsession, it can't cast spells or use abilities that target an area (such as the *fireball* spell) unless a source of its obsession is in that area. 
> *  The creature can't willingly move away from a source of its obsession.

\columnbreak

## Fel Dwarf Artificers


Among the merciless society of the Fel Dwarfs, infernal artificers exemplify the cruel ingenuity born from such a culture. Only the demonsmiths and sorcerer-prophets surpass them in crafting weapons of suffering. These hellish artisans are vital to the ceaseless creation of war machines that fuel the ambitious conquests of the Fel Dwarfs.

Although they do not wield brutal sorcery like their demonsmith masters, Fel Dwarf artificers possess extensive knowledge of the dark arts, alchemy, and metallurgy. They use this expertise to fabricate handheld versions of their unholy siege weapons—devices so cursed that only the artificers themselves can wield them. While it may seem that these contraptions are restricted to their creators to prevent their use against the Fel Dwarfs, the dark truth is far grimmer. During the forging process, a portion of the artificer’s soul is bound to the weapon, creating a dangerous connection. Even after the weapon's completion, its use erodes the wielder's soul and body, with prolonged exposure leading to horrific mutations or gradual petrification from within.


### Doomquake Battle Smith
The doomquake battle maul incorporates the devastating power of the doomquake mortar into a two-handed weapon. Artificers who wield these weapons in battle are a destructive force, capable of toppling stone walls with shockwaves that ripple out from every strike, pulverizing everything in their path. These weapons can also be slammed into the ground, causing the earth to buckle and shatter. Those unfortunate enough to stand on the unstable surface are swallowed by a churning sea of roiling earth.

### Magma Cannoneer
Using the same twisted alchemy as the magma cannon, magma spewers deliver gruesome deaths on the battlefield. These contraptions consist of a canister strapped to the artificer's back, constantly emitting black fumes and a sinister hissing sound. Tubes extend from the canister to a large nozzle, held by the artificer, which sprays molten metal capable of engulfing an entire regiment in seconds.

\columnbreak

### Deathscreecher Artillerist
Deathscreecher artillerists bombard their enemies with blasphemous ammunition derived from deathscreecher rockets. These Fel Dwarfs load handheld mortars with demolition rockets capable of felling a hill giant on impact. Alternatively, they can choose the more wicked dreadfire rockets, which release a hord of burning souls. These spectral entities howl through the air, seeking out the nearest living beings to incinerate.



### Fel Dwarf Artificer Tactics

The following tips can help you run fel dwarf artificers in combat more effectively:

#### Doomquake Battle Smith
* Excels at disrupting melee combatants and pairs well with ranged allies.
* Uses Doomquake Slam to create difficult terrain:
    * Position the slam so enemies are near the far edge of the area as this hinders enemy movement toward the battle smith and allies.

#### Magma Spewer
* Targets tightly packed groups of enemies, especially those with low Dexterity.
* Performs exceptionally well in chokepoints:
    * Use Resolute Stance to hold the line effectively.

#### Deathscreecher Artillerist
* Seeks opportunities to:
    *  Target three or more enemies.
    *  Target at least two enemies with low Wisdom using the Frighten ability.
* Forces enemies within 60 feet of the cannon to flee.
    *  Be cautious not to let allies pursue fleeing enemies into danger.
*  Uses Demolition Rocket in situations where crowd control is not possible.

> ## Fel Dwarf Doomquake Battle Smith
> *Medium humanoid (dwarf), lawful evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 17 (splint)  
> - **Hit Points** 105 (14d8 + 42)  
> - **Speed** 25 ft.  
> ___
> |STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
> |:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
> |14 (+2)|8 (-1)|16 (+3)|16 (+3)|12 (+1)|10 (+0)|
> ___
> - **Saving Throws** Strength +5, Intelligence +6  
> - **Damage Resistances** fire, poison  
> - **Skills** Arcana +6, Athletics +5, Land Vehicles +3, Smith's Tools +3, Tinker's Tools +3  
> - **Senses** darkvision 30 ft., passive Perception 10  
> - **Languages** Dwarvish, Infernal, Goblin, Orc  
> - **Challenge** 6 (2,300 XP)   &emsp;&emsp; **Proficiency Bonus** +3  
> ___
> 
> ***Debilitating Strikes.*** When the dwarf reduces a creature to 0 hit points with an attack, that creature is stunned for the next minute, even if it regains hit points.  
> 
> ***Dwarven Resilience.*** The dwarf has advantage on saving throws against poison.  
> 
> ***Siege Monster.*** The dwarf deals double damage to objects and structures.  
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Multiattack.*** The dwarf makes two attacks.  
> 
> ***Doomquake Maul.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage plus 9 (2d8) fire damage. If the target is a creature, it becomes sheathed in booming energy until the start of the dwarf's next turn. If the target willingly moves 5 feet or more before then, it takes 9 (2d8) thunder damage, and the energy dissipates.  
> 
> ***Doomquake Slam (3/Day).*** The dwarf slams the ground with its Doomquake Maul, creating a 15-foot cube of burning earth originating from itself. Each creature in the cube must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage, 9 (2d8) fire damage, and is knocked prone. On a success, a creature takes half damage and isn't knocked prone. The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain until cleared and the area vibrates violently until the start of the dwarf's next turn. At that point, the vibrations end in a calamitous burst, and each creature in the area must make a DC 14 Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, a creature takes 14 (4d6) thunder damage. On a success, it takes half damage. 
>
> ### Bonus Actions  
> ***Resolute Stance.*** At the start of the dwarf’s turn, it becomes Resolute* until the start of its next turn.
>
> **New condition described below*



### Doomquake Battle Smith Upgrade

The Doomquake Battle Smith can have any of the following upgrades. Its challenge rating (CR) increases by 1 for each upgrade it has.

##### Doomquake Battle Smith Upgrade
| d3  | Upgrade |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Deafening Thunder.***  Creatures sheathed in booming energy or standing in a vibrating area are deafened and cannot cast spells with a verbal component. A creature that fails a Constitution saving throw suffers these effects until the end of the dwarf’s next turn.
|  2  | ***Shockwave.***  The area of the Doomquake Slam increases to a 60-foot-long, 15-foot-wide line originating from the dwarf.
|  3  | ***Seismic Snare.*** When a creature fails the Dexterity saving throw against the Doomquake Slam while standing on earth or stone, it becomes buried as it sinks into the ground. A buried creature is restrained. Any creature, including the buried one, can use its action to free the buried creature by succeeding on a DC 14 Strength check. A creature making this check has advantage if it has a burrow speed.|

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
> * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
> * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
The Resolute condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

\pagebreakNum

> ## Fel Dwarf Magma Cannoneer
> *Medium humanoid (dwarf), lawful evil*  
> ___
> - **Armor Class** 17 (splint)  
> - **Hit Points** 97 (13d8 + 39)  
> - **Speed** 25 ft.

### Magma Cannoneer Upgrade

The magma cannoneer can have any of the following upgrades. Its challenge rating (CR) increases by 1 for each upgrade it has.

##### Magma Cannoneer Upgrade
| d3  | Upgrade |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1  | ***Caustic Tar.*** A creature ignited by the Magma Spray ability is also restrained. If a creature uses its action to douse the flames, it must succeed on a DC 14 Strength check. On a failure, the flames are not extinguished.
|  2  | ***Pyretic Acid.***  The magma cannoneer now deals acid damage instead of fire damage. Whenever a creature takes acid damage from the magma cannoneer, any armor it is wearing takes a permanent and cumulative -1 penalty to the AC it offers. Armor reduced to an AC of 10 is destroyed.
|  3  | ***Stinking Sulfur.*** Each creature that starts its turn within 5 feet of an ignited creature must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution saving throw or be poisoned until the end of its current turn. While poisoned in this way, the creature is Dazed (see new condition) and can’t cast spells other than cantrips.|

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Dazed 
> A dazed creature can’t take reactions and on its turn, it must choose whether it gets a move, an action, or a bonus action; it gets only one of the three.

The rocket explodes, creating a 15-foot-radius sphere of burning souls. At the start of the dwarf's next turn, the sphere moves up to 30 feet toward the nearest creature before disappearing. 
>

On a failed save, the creature takes 10 (3d6) fire damage and is frightened until the end of the dwarf's next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage and isn't frightened. If the creature fails the save by 5 or more (DC 9), it can't take reactions and must take the Dash action on each of its turns to move away from the sphere by the safest available route. 
> 
> The dwarf can't use this ability again until it reloads the launcher as an action or by using its Resolute Stance feature (no action required).

\pagebreakNum

## Ashsworn Elders
*Dwarven Clerics Serving Fiery Gods of Tyranny*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Relics <br>

Within the foreboding depths of the dark iron cathedrals, all manner of atrocious sacrifices, iniquitous spells, and blasphemous prayers are evoked. These rites are performed so that the dread gods of the world’s deepest recesses may be communed with, allowing the faithful to bargain for even the smallest portion of their unholy magics.

The wretched practitioners who dwell within these accursed halls are the Fel Dwarf Ashsworn. Within the Fel Dwarf empire, they are the ones who hoard the most foul rites and incantations, keeping the altars of their avaricious deities placated with meat, bone, and souls. The Ashsworn rank second in the Fel Dwarf hierarchy, standing just below the demon smiths. Though both orders clash constantly as they vie for power and status within Fel Dwarf society’s ruthless ranks.

\columnbreak

<br><br><br>

Unlike their demon smith brethren, the Ashsworn do not rely on blasphemous ingenuity to invent new weapons and spells. Instead, they beseech their hellish masters directly through dark hymns and blood rituals, pleading for raw might. That power becomes terrifyingly evident when these clerics leave their temples and march onto the fields of war. With a guttural cry and the raising of their onyx stone hammers, they draw forth the burning blood of the earth and hurl it at their foes, searing away skin and flesh in an agonizing death.

So dreadful are these conduits of destruction and suffering that even fellow Fel Dwarfs fear to fall before them. In their presence, warriors have been known to withstand wounds that would have slain them otherwise. Yet, just like the demon smiths, such malign practices come at a terrible cost. The magics of their heartless masters slowly turn the Ashsworn’s bodies to stone, trapping them eventually in a living prison of rock.

\pagebreakNum

### Fel Dwarf Ash Sworn Tactics

The primary goal of an Ashsworn is to secure a position where they can become Resolute, increasing the damage of their spell attacks and activating their Aura of Fanaticism, which bolsters nearby Fel Dwarfs.

Once established, the Ashsworn employs the following spells according to battlefield conditions:

**Magmatic Stone.**
Used when enemies are beyond the range of the Ashsworn's other offensive spells. The Ashsworn targets the front of an enemy formation whenever possible, increasing the likelihood that the storm created by the spell will pass through additional targets and hinder the enemy's advance.

**Bellows Breath.**
Bellows Breath is used as soon as two or more enemy creatures are within range. Friendly Fel Dwarfs will often position themselves to be affected by the breath as well, allowing them to benefit from its effects. This spell is particularly effective when combined with Shard Storm, as it enhances the damage dealt by subsequent attacks.

**Shard Storm.**
Shard Storm is typically cast after Bellows Breath whenever it can strike two or more enemies within range. The spell takes advantage of the bonuses granted by Bellows Breath, allowing the Ash Sworn to maximize its damage against clustered foes.

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
>  * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
>  * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
> 
> The condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

### New Spells

#### Bellows Breath
*5th-level Evocation (Artificer, Cleric, Paladin)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** Self (50-foot Cone)
- **Components:** V
- **Duration:** Instantaneous

You breathe out the fires of creation, reforging your allies and searing your foes. Each creature in a 40-foot Cone must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 3d10 Fire damage and is Tempered until the end of your next turn. While Tempered, the creature takes an extra 1d6 Fire damage whenever it is hit by an attack.

You may choose up to three creatures within the area to automatically succeed on this saving throw, taking no damage. You and those creatures are instead Heated until the end of your next turn. While Heated, whenever a creature hits another creature with an attack, it deals an extra 1d6 Fire damage on that hit.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** The initial damage increases by 1d10 for each slot level above 5th.

#### Magmatic Stone
*3rd-level Evocation (Sorcerer, Wizard)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** 100 feet
- **Components:** V, S, M (a bit of igneous rock)
- **Duration:** 1 round

You conjure a molten rock and hurl it at a creature within range. Make a ranged spell attack against the target. On a hit, the target takes 4d6 Bludgeoning damage. Whether the attack hits or misses, the rock explodes on impact, creating a 15-foot-radius sphere of whirling magma centered on the point of impact. Its area is Lightly Obscured and Difficult Terrain. The sphere spreads around corners and seeps through openings at least 5 feet in diameter.

At the start of your next turn, the sphere moves 30 feet in the direction you hurled it and dissipates. Any creature in the sphere when it first appears, and any creature whose space the sphere enters or passes through, must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 4d6 Fire damage. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** The Bludgeoning and Fire damage both increase by 1d6 for each slot level above 3rd.

\columnbreak

#### Shard Storm
*4th-level Evocation (Sorcerer, Wizard)*
___
- **Casting Time:** Action
- **Range:** 50 feet (50-foot Cone)
- **Components:** V, S
- **Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 minute
-  **Classes:**  Artificer

You send a wave of burning rock shards from your fingertips flying in a 50-foot Cone. Choose up to five creatures you can see in the cone. Make a ranged spell attack against each target. On hit, target takes 3d8 Piercing damage and a shard embeds itself in the creature. At the end of each of the creature's turns until the spell ends, it takes 2d8 Fire damage if the shard is still embedded.

**Removing the Shard.** A creature within reach can use an action to make a Wisdom (Medicine) check against your spell save DC to remove the shard. On a success, the shard is removed. On a failure, the shard is still removed, but the creature takes 1d8 Slashing damage.

***Using a Higher-Level Spell Slot.*** You may target one additional creature for each spell slot level above 4.

\pagebreakNum

## Bull Centaur Abominations
*Unholy Fusions of Dwarf, Bull, & Iron*<br>
**Habitat:** Desert, Grasslands, Mountain, Underdark, Urban, Volcanic; **Treasure:** Armaments <br>

Many daemonsmiths devise their own twisted methods to create bull centaurs when their numbers are too few to serve the needs of their malevolent plots. Yet the most ambitious among the fel artificers are never satisfied. They continue to subject these creatures to horrific experiments in pursuit of greater power.

The rare few that survive such brutal trials are known as abominations. Their hides are fused with metal, etched with hellish runes, and the blood of countless unholy creatures courses through their veins. 

\columnbreak

<br><br><br><br><br>

Infernal spearguns replace their hands—used to impale any who draw near or to launch burning projectiles at distant foes. Their hooves strike with such fury that they shatter the earth and ignite the very air, leaving rubble and broken bodies scattered about them.

### Bull Centaur Abomination Tactics
The abomination uses Cataclysmic Slam as soon as it can hit two or more targets. It prioritizes using Infernal Skewer against enemies who are Paralyzed or Frightened.

##### Bull Centaur Abominations Art Credit
* Burning Oil Well at Night - James Hamilton

> ### Traits
> ***Revel in Fear.*** The bull centaur's attacks score a critical hit on a roll of 10–20 against creatures that are Frightened.
>
> ### Actions
> ***Multiattack.*** The bull centaur makes two attacks, each with a different weapon.
>
> ***Infernal Skewer.*** *Melee or Ranged Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit, reach 10 ft. or range 60/90 ft., one target. *Hit:* 19 (2d12 + 6) piercing plus 13 (2d12) fire damage. On a critical hit, it deals an additional 13 (2d12) fire damage.
>
> ***Overwhelming Stomp.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one prone or smaller creature. *Hit:* 32 (4d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage and the target must succeed on a *Wisdom Saving Throw:* DC 19. <br>
>
> *Failure:* Target is Frightened until the end of the bull centaur's next turn. <br> 
> *Failure by 5+:* It also can’t take reactions and must Dash away from the bull centaur by the safest route each turn.
>
> ***Pulverizing Gore.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 24 (4d8 + 6) piercing damage and the target must succeed on a *Strength Saving Throw:* DC 19. <br> 
> *Failure:* The target is knocked Prone and cannot stand until the end of its next turn unless it spends all movement to make a DC 19 Constitution saving throw. On a success, it stands up.
>
> ***Cataclysmic Slam (1/Day).*** Each creature in a 30-foot cube originating from the bull centaur must make a *Strength or Dexterity Saving Throw (target’s choice):* DC 19. <br>
> *Failure:* 22 (4d10) thunder plus 22 (4d10) fire damage, and the target is knocked Prone. <br>
> *Failure by 5+:* The target is also Paralyzed until the end of the bull centaur’s next turn. <br>
> *Success:* Half damage only. <br>
> The ground in the area becomes difficult terrain if it's loose earth or stone.
>
> ### Bonus Actions
> ***Resolute Charge.*** At the start of its turn, bull centaur may move up to its speed in a straight line and becomes Resolute*.
>
> ### Reactions
> ***Retaliating Fire.*** *Trigger:* A creature 15–90 ft. away deals damage to the bull centaur. <br> *Response:* The bull centaur makes an Infernal Skewer attack against the attacking creature. The attack automatically misses if there is a hostile creature within 5 ft. of the bull centaur that is not Incapacitated.

\pagebreakNum

### Bull Centaur Abomination Loot
Most of the valuable goods that can be looted from bull centaurs are the remains of the equipment they were using or valuables they were carrying or hiding nearby. Most dropped or hidden valuables can be found using **Intelligence (Investigation)** or **Wisdom (Perception)** checks. 

Alternatively, you can roll for the loot randomly using a flat modifier.

##### Bull Centaur Abomination Loot Table  (Avg. 1,250gp)


| Looting Check Result or 1d20 + 7 |  Loot  |
|:---:|:-----------:|
|  1-9  | 1 500gp Items |
|  10-14 | 1d4(2) 500gp Items | 
|  15-21 | 1 1,000gp Items  |  
|  22-25 | 1d4(2) 1,000gp Items |  
|  26+ |  1 2,000gp Items |  

#### Optional: Describing Looted Materials
If you’re not interested in the specifics, simply say the party collected materials worth the rolled gp value. If you want more detail, the tables below suggest materials for different gp values by creature. They also provide **Example Names** and **Quality Descriptors** for items worth **half** or **twice** the table's gp value.

For example, a 1,000gp Bone item from a bull centaur could be a Abominable Centaur Horn, a 500gp version might be a Damaged Abominable Centaur Horn, and a 2,000gp version could be an Enhanced Abominable Centaur Horn.

***When To Use Descriptions.*** You can describe these items during looting, when selling them, or when crafting, but it's best not to make players track long descriptive names. Only use as much detail as is needed when needed.

##### Bull Centaur Abomination Materials: 1,000gp 

|1d4 | Material | Example Name (Quality Descriptors) | 
|:--:|:---:|:-----------:|
| 1   | Bone|   Abominable Centaur Horn (Damaged/Enhanced) | 
| 2   | Jewelry | Fire Opal (Damaged/Flawless)|  
| 3-4   | Metal|  Abominable Centaur Plating (Damaged/Masterwork) |  

#### Optional: Magic Loot for Item Crafting
If you plan to use harvested loot for crafting magic items, you can try to thematically align the effects of the magic items being crafted with the creature's they're harvested from. In the case of bull centaurs, Fiendish effects would make the most sense. These could be magic item effects that steal the lives of their victims or conjure unholy magic.

***Magic Loot GP Values.*** The value of magic ingredients depends on your crafting system. Subtract their cost from the normal loot’s gp value. If the ingredient is worth significantly more, subtract it from the next treasure hoard instead.

\columnbreak

> ##### New Condition: Resolute
> The creature stubbornly digs in to weather any assault. It experiences the following effects:
> * Attack rolls made against the creature have disadvantage.
> * The creature can't take its move action on its turn.
> * The creature makes Strength checks and all saving throws with advantage, except Dexterity saving throws, which it makes with disadvantage.
The Resolute condition ends if an effect moves the creature against its will, knocks it prone, or the creature becomes charmed, frightened, or incapacitated. A creature that is prone, charmed, frightened, or incapacitated can't become Resolute.

\pagebreakNum

## Doom Cannons
A terrifying fusion of demon and war machine, the doom cannon stands as the pinnacle of fel dwarf siegecraft. This nightmarish engine is created by binding the soul of a powerful demon into brass and iron. The imprisoned spirit grants the cannon a horrifying semblance of life—groaning and shaking with unearthly hatred, yearning only for destruction.

These cursed weapons are overseen by sadistic engineers, typically fel dwarfs, who do not merely crew the doom cannon but rather guide it as one would a rabid beast. They feed the war machine freshly slain bodies, shoveling corpses into its rear chamber. Within the infernal mechanism, flesh and bone are swiftly liquefied, their souls extracted to fuel its chaotic flames. These unnatural fires erupt from the cannon’s maw,  scorching the battlefield and warping anything unfortunate enough to be caught in their wake.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

Among the deadliest instruments in the fel dwarfs’ arsenal, doom cannons are often sold to unholy warbands in exchange for vast hoards of slaves or plundered wealth. Tribes of fiend-worshipping marauders and orders of dark knights frequently employ them as artillery in their accursed crusades. However, those who wield these monstrous weapons must eventually pay the true price of their power.

Even with experienced handlers, the safeguards meant to contain the demon within inevitably fail. Many doom cannons have surged forward into enemy lines, abandoning their handlers in fits of uncontrollable bloodlust. At other times, the chaotic energies within grow unstable, causing deadly magical disruptions or leading to catastrophic explosions that consume all nearby—friend and foe alike. Some turn upon their own masters, scarring their minds with unspeakable horrors—or worse, drag them screaming into their fiery cores, where their souls remain trapped within the infernal machine.


___
___
> ## Doom Cannon
> *Huge fiend construct, chaotic evil*  
> ___
> - Armor Class 18 (natural armor)  
> - Hit Points 187 (15d12 + 90)  
> - Speed 40 ft.

> ___

> - **Damage Resistances** cold, lightning  
> - **Damage Immunities** poison, fire  
> - **Condition Immunities** charmed, exhaustion, frightened, paralyzed, petrified, poisoned

> ***Bound Demon.*** The cannon is magically bound to three other creatures. At the start of each of the cannon's turns during combat, each of the bound creatures within 5 feet of the cannon must make a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw to maintain control of it. If none of the creatures succeed on the saving throw or no creatures make a saving throw, roll on the Doom Cannon Mishap Table (found at the end of the document).  
> 
> ***Consumes Corpse.*** The cannon or another creature can use its action to feed the corpse of a Medium or Small creature that died within the last 24 hours and is within 5 feet of the cannon into it. The creature’s corpse, along with all nonmagical items it is wearing or carrying, is destroyed, and its soul is trapped within the cannon until the cannon dies. While a creature’s soul is trapped, it cannot be resurrected.  
>
> ***Heavy Treads.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws and checks made against effects that would knock it prone or move it against its will. It must spend all of its movement to stand up and cannot squeeze through spaces.  
> 
> ***Immutable Form.*** The cannon is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.  
> 
> ***Magic Resistance.*** The cannon has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.  
> 
> ### Actions  
> 
> ***Ram.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 25 (3d12 + 6) bludgeoning damage. If the cannon moved at least 20 feet straight toward the target immediately before the attack, it deals an additional 19 (3d12) bludgeoning damage. If this attack kills a creature, the cannon can use its Consumes Corpse feature as a bonus action.  
> 
> ***Fire! (Recharges when the cannon consumes a corpse or after a long rest).*** The cannon fires a bolt of chaos flames at a point 30 to 600 feet away from itself. Each creature within 15 feet of that point must make a DC 18 Dexterity saving throw, taking 36 (8d8) fire damage on a failed save, or half as much on a success. This damage ignores fire resistance. If a creature fails the saving throw by 5 or more, it suffers a mutation* (Mutation Save DC 18).
>

### Ice-Forged Doom Cannon
In the cursed, frostbitten lands where malevolent forces hold sway, the fel dwarfs devised a chilling variant of the doom cannon: the ice-forged doom cannon. Unlike its fiery counterpart, this dreadful siege engine is crafted by subjugating the living nightmares that haunt the frozen wastes.

When fed a corpse, the engine grinds the body into crimson ice shards, transmuting the remains into its deadly ammunition.

\columnbreak

<br><br>

It then unleashes them in a merciless barrage of unholy frost. This accursed ammunition mercilessly drains the warmth and vitality from its victims, leaving only frozen husks behind.

Many savage barbarian tribes dwelling in these forsaken lands pay exorbitant sums to acquire such weapons, and survivors of an ice-forged doom cannon’s onslaught often find themselves swiftly overwhelmed by zealots of the dark gods.

### Doom Cannon of Damnation
The doom cannon of damnation is an even more powerful iteration of the doom cannon—an exceedingly rare and perilous creation, requiring the greatest mastery of dark artificers. Unlike its predecessors, this monstrous weapon does not merely rain fire or ice upon its enemies; instead, it unleashes torrents of soul-rending energy.

Those caught in its malignant blasts have their very essence wrenched from their bodies, their souls drawn into the cannon to fuel its devastating black magic. A single doom cannon of damnation can reap the souls of entire armies, trapping them within its iron frame to perpetuate its insatiable hunger.

\columnbreak

<br>

Yet, for all its devastating power, even the most ruthless warlords hesitate to wield such a weapon. Should the demon within ever break free, the consequences would be catastrophic. There are whispers of a battle where one such cannon escaped its handlers' control, feasting indiscriminately upon all combatants. When the dust settled, nothing remained of the armies that had clashed—only the doom cannon of damnation itself, rolling onward across the land, with the souls of every fallen warrior stowed within its depths.
